15 Fathers and mothers have her in horror, because it is she who corrupts their children. Even women curse and insult her, because it is she who has distanced their sons from them. Stone thrown in the middle of the street, on which everyone stumbles, but where many return. Cause of the struggle of the strong; cause of the fall of the weak. Friend of darkness, she takes pleasure in obscurity. Congener of the night, pleasure that engenders shame. Evil desire, whose accomplishment is perdition. Defilement of thoughts and members. Degradation of intelligence. Extinction of judgment. Perpetual drunkenness. Occasions of divine and human combats. Diminution of the strength of members. Depravation of intelligence in its thoughts
ܡܚܒܠܬ ܗܘܢܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܀ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܐܢܫ ܢܐܡܪ ܥܠ ܙܢܝܬܐ ܒܨܝܪ ܡܢ ܡܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܐܡܿܪ ܥܠܝܗܿ. ܒܝܫܬܐ ܗܝ ܓܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܣܘܓܐܐ ܕܒܝܫ̈ܬܐ ܟܢܝܫܢ ܒܗܿ. ܠܗܕܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܒܝܫܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܝܫܘܥ ܕܢܬܠ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܕܟܕ ܠܗܿ ܫܒ̣ܼܩ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܢܥܒܕܗܿ. ܘܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܒܗܿ ܝܘܠܦܢܝܐ ܠܒܺܝܫܐ ܕܐܟܘܬܗܿ ܢܗܘܐ. ܡܘܗܒܬܗ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܦܪܨܘܦܐ ܚܕ ܗܘܝܐܼ ܢܝܫܗ̇ ܠܘܬ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܪܵܗܿܛ. ܐܝܟ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܒܝ ܩܕܡܐ ܢܚ̇ܘܐ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܟܠܗܿ ܡܓܪܬ ܪ̈ܘܚܗ. ܠܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܕܢܗܝܡܢܘܢ ܒܗ ܀
16 And the more one speaks of the prostitute, the less one will say what she is. She is the supreme evil through the multitude of evils gathered within her. Now, it is to her that Jesus wants to grant the forgiveness of all this evil, so that, having forgiven her, He might make of her a model for others; and that the grace which is in her might be a teaching for the wicked who are like her. For the gift of Christ, while it is for a single person, runs toward the goal for a great number, in such a way that in me first, Jesus Christ manifests all His long-suffering, as an example for those who will have to believe in Him. The greatest miracle.
ܠܗܕܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܡܫܖܪܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܠܗ̇ ܐܣܓܝ ܝܫܘܥ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ. ܬܡܝܗ ܗܘܐ ܚܘܠܡܢܗ ܕܡܫܖ̈ܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܿܩܪܒ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܒܥܪ̈ܣܐ. ܪܒܐ ܗܘܬ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܘܐܦ ܕܗܿܘ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ
17 In her who was so totally paralyzed by the sickness of sin, Jesus multiplied the healing. Stupefying is the health restored to the paralytic who was presented to Jesus on a stretcher. Great also was the healing of that other one who for thirty-eight years lay on a bed. Sublime and glorious was the help


[p. 476] {#po.41.p476}
ܘܬܡܢܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܒܥܪܣܐ ܡܿܓܣ ܗܘܐ. ܡܥܠܝ ܗܘܐ ܘܫܒܝܚ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܫܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܚܓܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܫܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܢܗ ܡܬܝܼܗܒ ܗܘܐ. ܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܪܒܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܕ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܢ ܇ ܒܦܚܡܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ̇ ܕܗܕܐ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܀
still given by Him to other lame and paralyzed people. But these (healings), although important, had no importance in comparison to the greatness of this healing of the sinful woman.
ܡܫܖܪܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܐܬܐܣܝܬ. ܗܿܝ ܕܠܘ ܗܕܡܺܝܗܿ ܓ̈ܠܝܐ ܟܪ̈ܝܗܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܡܼܪܦܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܗ݈ܝ ܢܦܫܗܿ ܘܚܘܫ̈ܒܝܗ̇ ܒܬܡܣܘܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܡܹܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܐܢܫ ܠܗܕܐ ܩ̇ܪܐ ܡܫܖܪܿܝܬ ܒܢܦܫܗܿ. ܡܛܘܠ ܕܚܠܦ ܦܓܪܗ̇ ܢܦܫܗܿ ܡܢ ܥܘܙܗܿ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܡܫܖ̈ܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܀ ܘܣܒܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܣܡܗܿ ܝܫܘܥ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܓܒܪ̈ܝܗܿ ܣܓܝܐ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܟܕ ܒܥܠܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗܿ. ܘܒܟܠ ܡܿܙܢܝܐ ܗܘܬ. ܟܕ ܒܪ ܢܩܦܐ ܕܫܘܬܦܘܬܐ ܫܖ̈ܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܐ ܩܢܝܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܘܐܦ ܒܖܝܬܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ̈ܠܗܐ ܣܓ̈ܝܐܐ ܦܠܚܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܠܗܗ̇ ܠܐ ܝܕܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܟܕ ܓܝܪ̈ܝܗܿ ܦܬܟܪ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܡܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܒܒܪ ܫܘܬܦܘܬܐ ܕܫܪ̈ܪ̈ܐ ܠܐ
18 She was healed all at once, the paralyzed woman, she whose visible limbs were not sick nor weakened, but whose soul and thoughts lay in the rot of sin. And it is with reason that one calls this one a paralytic of the soul, because, instead of her body, it was her soul which, through the violence of sin, was paralyzed. 19 And I think that Jesus also applied the figure of the pagan woman to the sinful woman. Like the latter, she had had several men, but had no husband. She was entirely corrupted, not having a spouse of true communion; pagan, likewise, because she served many gods, while she did not know Him who is her God, and because, her idolatrous adulteries being innumerable, she was ignorant of the yoke of true union.
ܡܦܣܐ ܗܘܬ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܗܿܘ ܡܖܝܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ ܐܣܝ ܠܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ. ܐܣܝ ܒܩܕܡܐ ܠܙܢܝܬܐ ܒܫܘܒܩܢܗ. ܘܠܐܚܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܒܖܪܝܬܐ ܒܙܩܝܦܗ. ܥܒܼܕ ܠܗܕܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܡܢ ܛܡܐܬܐ. ܘܠܗܿܝ ܡܝܩܖܬ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܡܢ ܣܓܕܬ ܠܐ̈ܠܗܐ. ܠܐ ܓܒܪ̈ܝܗ̇ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ ܡܬܡܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܠܐ ܐ̈ܠܗܝܗܿ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܡܬܚܫܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܠܐ ܠܗܕܐ ܒܥܠܐ ܕܫܪܪ̈ܐ. ܘܠܐ ܠܗܿܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܩܘܫܬܐ. ܠܐ ܗܕܐ ܡܢ ܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܠܐ ܗܿܝ ܡܢ ܕܚܠܬ ܦܬܟܪ̈ܐ. ܣܒܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܀
20 But this Lord of both (women) healed both: He healed the first, the prostitute, by His forgiveness; and the second, the pagan, by His cross; the first, soiled as she was, He made holy; and the second, who worshipped gods, He made to honor God. The men of the prostitute, one could not count them; the gods of the pagan, one could not imagine them. No true husband for the first; no true God for the second. The one was not satisfied by debauchery; the other was not satiated by idolatry.
ܕܡܝܢ ܗܘܝ ܠܚܕ̈ܕܐ ܒܒܝܫܬܐ. ܘܐܬܕܡܝ ܠܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܒܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܗܘܬ ܙܢܝܚܬܐ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܠܐܣܝܘܬܗܿ ܕܒܪܝܬܐ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܚܘܠܡܢܗ̇ ܕܡܣܪ̈ܚܬܐ ܆ ܢܣܒ ܣܒܪܐ ܥܠ ܐܣܝܘܬܗܿ ܕܡܛܢܦܬܐ. ܘܡܢ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ ܢܬܡܗ ܒܪܒܘܬ ܚܟܡܬܗ ܕܡܐܣܝܝܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܠܐ ܢܕܬ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܢܩܦܗܿ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ. ܠܐ ܣܥܛ ܡܢ ܐܣܝܘܬܗܿ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܒܚܢܛܝܬܐ ܡܥܼܢܝܒܠܐ ܗܘܬ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܣܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܿܝ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ̇ ܢܚܬ ܡܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܀ ܗܝ ܕܠܘ ܗܝ ܪ̈ܗܛܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܐܝܟ ܙܢܝܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܗܼܘ ܐܬܐ ܠܘܬܗܿ ܐܝܟ ܐܣܝܐ. ܡܢ ܒܬܖ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܚܕܐ. ܗܿܝ ܐܚܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܗܘܬ. ܐܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܘܬ ܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܪܗܛܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܙܢܝܬܐ. ܠܘܬ ܚܕܐ ܐܬܩܪܒ ܘܠܚܕܐ ܩܪܒ ܠܘܬ ܚܕܐ ܢܥܼܝܬ ܘܠܐܚܖܬܐ ܥܠܝ ܠܚܕܐ ܗܘ ܝܕܥ ܘܠܐܚܖ̈ܬܐ ܐܠܦ ܕܬܕܥܗ. ܠܘܬ ܒܖܪܝܬܐ ܐܬܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܩܪ̈ܬܗ. ܘܠܙܢܝܬܐ ܢܓܕ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܒܥܬܗ ܀
21 Similar to one another in evil, they resemble each other also in forgiveness. The prostitute is figured by the healing of the pagan, because he who sees the healing of the corrupted woman will draw from it hope for the healing of the impure one. And from the help (received) by both (women), he will be amazed at the greatness of the wisdom of Him who heals both. For He who had no repugnance at the touch of the prostitute, had no horror of healing the pagan, corrupted by sin. How, indeed, could He have rejected her for whom He had descended from heaven? 22 She who was not [a pagan] ran to Him as a prostitute, but He came to her as a physician, for after He had come to one, this other ran to Him. He had come to the pagan, but it is the prostitute who ran to Him. To the one, He was approached, but the other, He approached. To the one He descended, to the other He ascended. The one, He knew; to the other, He taught that she might know Him. To the pagan He came without her having called Him; the prostitute, He drew secretly, while she was not seeking Him.

### Secret dispositions
ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܩܪܝܬܐ ܒܒܝܬܗ ܕܫܡܥܘܢ ܡܫܬܡܫ ܗܘܐ. ܘܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܣܡ̈ܝܟܐ ܠܐ ܪܓܝܫ ܗܘܐ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܪܓܝܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܗܘܘ.
23 Thus was celebrated the mystery of the forgiveness of the guest in the house of Simon. And none of the guests had noticed it; and if they had not


[p. 478] {#po.41.p478}
ܘܡܝܬܐ ܒܡܕܡ ܕܣܥܪ ܚܝܐ ܠܐ ܪ̈ܓܫ. ܗܿܘ ܕܝܗܿܒ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܝܗܿܒ ܗܘܐ. ܘܗܿܝ ܕܢܣܒܐ ܚܘܣܝܐ ܢܣܒܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܣܡ̈ܝܟܐ ܠܐ ܡܗܝܡ̈ܢܐ ܠܗܕܐ ܟܠܗܿ ܛܒܬܐ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܥܒܝܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܡܙܡܢܝܐ ܟܕ ܦܬܘܪܗ ܒܡܐ̈ܟܠܬܐ ܥܬܝܪ ܗܘܐ ܆ ܢܦܫܗ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܐ ܡܣܟܝܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܓܠܝ̈ܬܐ ܡܬܚܙܐ ܡܝܩܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܒܟ̈ܣܝܬܐ ܫܝܘܛܐ. ܗܿܘ ܡܣܓܐ ܚܡܖܐ ܘܚܛܝܬܐ ܡܣܓܝܐ ܚܘܒܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܡܫܦܥ ܙܘ̈ܡܐ ܘܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ. ܗܿܘ ܡܬܩܢ ܦܬܘܪܗ ܘܙܢܝܬܐ ܡܬܩܢܐ ܘܡܛܝܒܐ ܢܦܫܗܿ. ܗܽܘ ܩܪܐ ܕܢܫܬܒܗܪ ܘܗܕ̈ܐ ܐܬܩܪܝܬ ܕܬܙܕܕܩ. ܗܘ ܩܪܐ ܠܡܠܦܝܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܐܬܩܪܝܬ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܀
noticed, it is because they were dead, and a dead person feels nothing of what a living person does. He who gives forgiveness was giving. She who receives mercy was receiving; and the guests, who did not have faith, were strangers to all this good action. Although, from the host, the table was rich in dishes, the soul was poor in love: outwardly he seemed honorable, but in the secret he was despicable. The one multiplies wine, the sinful woman multiplies love. The one makes the jars overflow; the sinful woman, her tears. The one arranges his table; the prostitute arranges and prepares her soul. The one invites to be praised; she is invited to be justified. The one invites the Master; she is invited by God.
ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܐܝܩܪܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܣܓܝ ܗܘܐ. ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܟܣܝܢ ܚܘܒܗܿ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ ܡܝܬܪ ܗܘܐ. ܦܪܝܫܝܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܟ ܪܚܡ ܫܘܒܗܪܐ ܘܪܿܐܓ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܐܢܫܝܐ. ܗܝ ܕܡܙܡܢܐ ܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܥܒܿܕ ܗܘܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܒܓܠܝ̈ܬܐ ܐܣܝܪ ܘܠܘ ܒܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ. ܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܟܣ̈ܝܬܐ ܚܐܪ̈. ܐܣܬܪ̈ܩ ܓܝܪ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܣܦܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ. ܐܦ ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܗܝܡܢܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܒܬܪ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܒ̈ܢܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܥܩܒ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܡ ܡܫܟܚܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܡܗܝܡܢܘ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܚܕ ܡܩܒܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܚܕ
24 In appearance, the honor of the Pharisee increased; in the secret, the love of the prostitute was enriched. The Pharisee, like one who loves vanity and seeks human glory, made the invitation procuring this human glory, for human glory is linked to appearances, but not to what is secret. The glory that comes from God, one perceives it in the secret. The (Pharisee) is deprived of love, because he is empty of faith. Thus he cannot have faith, he who is in quest of human glory. How could you believe, you who take (your) glory from one another and who do not seek the glory that comes from God alone?
ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐ ܒܥܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܀ $ ܣܓܝ ܗܘܐ ܓܝܪ ܚܘܒܗ̇ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܕܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܣܗܕܘܬܗ ܕܡܪܢ. ܡܚܒ ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܚܘܒܐ ܐܢܫܝܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܕܡܠܬܗ ܘܢܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܠܦܢܘܬܗ. ܐܝܟ ܕܡܚܒ ܐܢܫ ܠܐܝܢܐ ܕܨܒܿܐ ܕܢܐܬܪ̈ ܡܢܗ ܒܡܠܬܐ. ܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܠܦܢܐ ܡܚܒܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ. ܘܡܛܘܠ ܗܕܐ ܒܚܡܫܡܐܐ ܕܝܥܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܬܡܬܠ ܚܘܒܗ̇ ܘܕܗܘ ܒܚܡܫܝܢ ܀
25 More abundant was the love of the prostitute than that of the Pharisee, by the testimony of Our Lord. But the Pharisee still loved Christ with a human love, because he saw in Him only the renown of His word and the brilliance of His teaching; like one who loves someone only because he wants to enrich himself from him in his word. But the sinful woman, she did not love Jesus as one (loves) a master, but as (one loves) God. That is why her love is estimated at five hundred denarii, and the other at fifty.
ܐܠܐ ܡܠܦܢܝܐ ܐܦܢ ܡܫܚܝܠܦܝܢ ܡܩ̈ܒܠܝܐ ܕܝܘܠܦܢܝܗ ܡܢ ܚܕ̈ܕܐ ܠܘܬ ܟܘܠܗܘܢ ܡܬܢܚܬ. ܟܕ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܢܗܝܪ ܒܡܕܥܗ ܝܗܿܒ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ. ܘܠܗܿܘ ܕܒܨܝܪ ܒܡܪ̈ܕܘܬܗ ܡܣܪܚ ܒܡܫܘܚܬܗ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܕܦܓܖܢܐܝܬ ܙܡܢܗ ܦܓܖ̈ܢܐܝܬ ܐܙܼܠ ܠܘܬܗ. ܘܠܐ ܟܠܐ ܡܢܗ ܡܐܙܠܬܗ. ܟܕ ܛܒ ܠܘ ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܥܒܕܗ̇ ܠܩܪܝܬܐ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܡܛܠܬܗ ܠܬܡܢ ܐܙܠ ܗܘܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܗܺܝ ܕܥܬܝܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܬܪ̈ܗܛ. ܐܠܐ ܛܟ ܐܦ ܡܛܠܬܗ. ܡܛܠ ܕܥܕܡܐ ܠܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܕܪܟ ܪܗܛ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܨܒܼܐ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܐܫܬܘܝ ܕܡܕܡ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܢܕܥ. ܘܐܟܪ ܕܡܣܟܢܐܝܬ ܗܝܡܢ ܐܦ ܡܣܟܢܐܝܬ ܐܫܟܚ. ܠܡܠܦܝܐ ܙܡܢ ܘܡܠܦܝܐ ܐܙܼܠ ܠܘܬܗ. ܠܒܪܢܫܐ ܙܕܝܩܐ ܩܪܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܥܠ ܠܓܘ ܒܝܬܗ. ܠܝܒܝܐ ܕܫܥܬܐ ܨܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܝܩܪ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܫܬܟܚ ܠܗ ܒܓܘ ܒܝܬܗ ܀
26 But the Master, although those who receive his teaching are different from one another, descended toward all; for, to him who has a brilliant mind, He gives great gifts; and to him who has a more humble conduct, He gives according to his measure. Likewise also, at the house of the Pharisee who invited Him corporally, He came to his house corporally; He did not refuse to go there, although it was without faith that (the other) had made his invitation. It was not for him that He (Christ) had come there, but for her who was preparing to run toward Him; perhaps also for him (Simon), who ran (toward Christ) to the limit of his understanding: he did not want to know more, he did not deserve (to know more): to the measure of his poor faith, he received poorly. He invited a doctor, and a doctor went to his house. He invited a just man, and such a (just man) entered his house. He wanted to do honor to the prophet of the hour, and in this way (Christ) found Himself in his house.


[p. 480] {#po.41.p480}
ܩܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܠܡ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܚܕ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܕܢܫܬܪܐ ܠܘܬܗ. ܗܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܬܫܥܝܬܗܿ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܘܟܕ ܛܒ ܡܘܕܥ ܪ̈ܫܗ ܕܫܖ̈ܒܐ ܕܥܠ ܦܪܝܫܝܐ ܡܫܬܥܐ. ܐܠܐ ܢܝܫܗ ܠܘܬ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܗܿ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ ܚܐܪ̈. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܒܛܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܡܟܬܒܢܐ. ܕܢܠܦܢܿ ܕܐܙܕܡܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܘܐܙܼܠ ܐܫܬܪ̈ܝ. ܐܠܐ ܕܢܠܦܢ ܡܪܚܡܢܘܬܗ ܘܕܟܠ ܕܩܪܒ ܠܘܬܗ ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܡܩܒܠ ܠܗ. ܘܕܨܒܐ ܕܢܫܒܘܩ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܠܒܢܝ̈ܢܝܫܐ. ܘܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܒܗܕܐ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܫܪܝ ܐܢܫܐܝܬ ܘܫܠܡ ܐܠܗܐܝܬ. ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܒܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܕܘܟ̈ܝܬܐ ܀
27 A Pharisee invited Jesus to descend to his house. Such is the beginning of the account of the sinful woman; and although the beginning of the story clearly indicates that it is a question of a Pharisee, nevertheless its design aims at the action of the sinful woman. For the writer does not care to teach us that Jesus was invited and that He went to descend (to his house), but to teach us His mercy, and that whoever approaches Him with faith, He receives him: and He wanted to forgive men their sins, and that it is not only in this place that He began as a man and ended as God, but in other places as well.

### Receiving Jesus as God
ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܦ ܒܩܛܢܐ ܐܙܕܡܢ ܠܡܫܬܘܬܐ ܘܟܬܝܒܐ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܬܕܡܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܕܗܘܬ ܒܗܿ. ܠܘܬ ܗܿܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܫܚܠܦ ܡ̈ܺܝܐ ܠܚܡܪ̈ܐ ܚܐܖ ܢܝܫܗܿ ܕܬܫܥܝܬܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܒܒܝܬܗ ܕܠܟܙܖ ܗܿܘ ܕܐܩܝܡ ܡܢ ܡܝܬ̈ܐ. ܐܬܩܢܘ ܠܗ ܚܫܡܝܬܐ ܕܢܠܥܣ. ܘܡܪܿܝܡ ܫܩܠܬ ܫܛܝܦܬܐ ܕܒܣܡܐ ܣܓܝ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ ܘܐܫܦܥܬ ܥܠ ܪܫܗ. ܘܟܬܝܒܐ ܚܫܡܝܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܗܢܐ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܀
28 Here is that indeed at Cana also He was invited to a wedding and the account was written because of the miracle which was realized there; for its goal in the account aims at the change of water into wine. Likewise in the house of Lazarus - he whom He raised from the dead -, a supper was prepared for Him so that He might eat. And Mary brought a vase of perfume of great price and poured it on His head. Now the supper was described because of this action.
ܡܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܡܛܠ ܫܘܒܩܢܗ̇ ܕܙܢܝܬܐ. ܐܥܠ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܩܪܝܗܝ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܕܢܠܥܣ ܒܒܝܬܗ. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܪܫܗ ܕܫܪ̈ܒܐ. ܦܘܚܡܐ ܡܩܕܡ ܡܥܬܕ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܐ. ܠܙܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ. ܐܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܦܪܝܫܝܐ ܠܘ ܕܢܠܥܣ ܒܒܝܬܗ ܩܖܐ̇ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܬܠ ܠܗ ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܦܐܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܬܠ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܙܢܝܬܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܩܪܒܬ ܠܘܬܗ. ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܚܡ ܠܗ ܫܐܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢܗ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܝܗܿ. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܩܢܛܪܘܢܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܝܕܥܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܝܫܘܥ ܒܥܼܐ ܕܢܐܙܠ ܠܒܝܬܗ. ܡܫܬܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܬܥܘܠ ܬܚܝܬ ܡܛܠܠܝ. ܐܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܟܠ ܦܫܝܩ ܠܗܼ
29 Here then also it is because of the forgiveness of the prostitute, that he introduces the account where the Pharisee invites Him to eat in his house, for the beginning of the story prepares a comparison: it camps the two characters, the prostitute and the Pharisee. For if the Pharisee had invited Him as God, it is not to eat in his house that he would have invited Him, but to give Him what it befits God to give. Likewise also the prostitute, when she approached Him, it is what befits Him that she asked of Him, the forgiveness of her sins. Look, the centurion: it is because he knew that He was God, when Jesus had wanted to come into his house, that he excused himself saying to Him: I am not worthy that you enter under my tent, but say a word, as it is easy for the God of all, and my child will be healed.
ܘܡܬܐܣܐ ܛܠܝܝ ܀ ܚܙܝܬ. ܕܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܩܖ̈ܝܗܝ. ܘܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܦ ܐܚܖ̈ܢܐ ܒ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܩܪܐ ܥܡܗ. ܙܢܝܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܩܪ̈ܒܬ. ܘܡܛܘܠ ܗܿܕܐ ܒܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܐ ܚܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܬ ܨܒܼܐ ܓܝܕ ܝܫܘܥ ܒܚܘܒܗ ܘܗܼܘܐ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܢܫܟܚܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܥܐ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܣܦܩ ܗܘܢܐ. ܢܣܒ ܡܢ ܡܠܝܘܬܗ ܐܝܬ ܕܪ̈ܘܖ̈ܒܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܕܡܨ̈ܥܝܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܕܙܥܘܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܀
30 Now you have seen that the Pharisee invited Him as a man; and because of this he invited also other men with Him. But the prostitute approached Him as God; and for that she looked at no one else; Jesus who wanted it in His love became man so that each one might find Him as he seeks Him and to the measure of the capacity of (his) mind. From His superabundance one receives (gifts), whether great, or medium, or smaller.
ܩܕܡܓܝܪ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܦܣ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ ܇ ܘܡܢ ܡܫܼܬܘܕܥܢܘܬܐ ܕܒ̈ܢܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܥܠܝ ܗܘܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܠܗܕ̈ܐ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܐܬܐ ܐܫܬܟܚ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܟܘܠ ܡܘ̈ܫܚܢ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܢܬܩܪܒܢ. ܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܒܪܢܫܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܠܗܐܝܬ ܡܬܪܥܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ. ܗܢܘܢ
31 For before He was man, one knew in Him only the nature of spiritual beings, and He was raised above the knowledge of men. But when, by His grace, He came into this (nature) here, a place was found for all categories to approach Him: men who esteemed as human what was divine, esteem it as divine; those who
ܕܣܒܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܙܕܝܩ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܿܢ ܕܡܫܟܚܝܢ ܒܗ ܕܡܘܬܗܘܢ. ܡ̈ܠܦܢܝܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܡܠܦܢܘܬܗ. ܥܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ. ܝܕܥ̈ܝ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܘܬ ܡܦܫ̈ܩܢܐ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ. ܡܟ̈ܣܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܚܐ ܪ ܗܘܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܒܐ̈ܦܐ ܦܨ̈ܝܚܬܐ. ܙܢܝ̈ܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܡܬܢܚܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬ ܫܘܒܩܢܗܝܢ ܀ ܗܕܝܘ̈ܛܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܡܠܓܠܓ ܗܘܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ. ܫܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܢܚܬ ܕܢܥܘܠ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܗܘܢ. ܡܡܚܝܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠܝ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܗ ܘܐܓܪ ܠܗܿ ܕܬܪ̈ܗܛ ܬܐܚܘܕ ܩܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܒܘܫܗ. ܪ̈ܚܡܝ ܫܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܚܘܝ ܢܦܫܗ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܬܩܪܐܿ. ܘܡܛܘܠ ܕܟܠ ܗܘ ܝܫܘܥ. ܐܦ ܠܟܠ ܟܠ ܗܘܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܝܟ ܗܝ ܕܟܬܝܒܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܟܠ ܒܟܠ. ܕܩܕܡ ܓܝܪ ܗܘܼܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܕܢܝܗܘܐ. ܥܬܝܕ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܒܝܬ ܟܘܢܫܐ ܕܟܠ. ܘܩܕܡ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܝܬ ܡܣܡܟ ܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܟܠ. ܙܡܢܗ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܕܢܠܥܣ ܒܒܝܬܗ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܒܗܕܐ ܐܤܣܒܪ ܕܡܢܝܚ ܠܗ. ܐܙܕܡܢ ܠܗ ܕܢܡܠܐ ܠܗ ܪ̈ܓܬܗ ܡܛܠ ܕܗܘܝܘ ܕܐܦ ܕܒܝܝ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܐܝܣܪ̈ܐܝܠ ܡܢ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܡܩܒܠ ܗܘܐ. ܘܢܘܩܝܐ ܘܪ̈ܣܣܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܫܩܠ ܗܘܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܣܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܠܗܠܝܢ ܣܢܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܘܠܗܠܝܢ ܒܥܿܐ ܗܘܐ ܀
esteemed themselves just, as if they found in Him their model; the doctors, because His teaching was richer than theirs; those who were versed in the law, as one of the interpreters of the law; the publicans, because He had looked at them with a serene face; the prostitutes, because He had lowered Himself to forgive them. 32 The simple, because He stammered with them and like them; the Samaritans, because He had descended to enter their city; the wounded woman, because He blocked her path toward Him and He permitted her to run to take the fringe of His garment; the friends of feasts, because He showed Himself like them invited also; and because Jesus is everything, He was everything to all, as it is written that God will be all in all. Indeed He was in advance what He was destined to become, for He was destined to become the place of gathering for all and, before having been at the feast, the head of all. The Pharisee invited Him to eat at his house. Thinking in this to please Him, He let Himself be invited by him, so that through Him his desire might be fulfilled, because it is He who, formerly, received even the sacrifices of Israel and accepted their libation and their sprinkling: they imagined indeed that He had need of it and that He asked it of them.
ܥܕܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܬܡܛܐ ܐܢܫ ܕܢܪ̈ܓܫ ܒܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܐܝܟ ܕܨܒܐ ܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܡܪܓܫ ܝܗܿܒ ܢܦܫܗ ܕܢܕܥܝܘܗܝ. ܡܛܘܠ ܕܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܡܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܕܢܕܥܝܘܗܝ ܠܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܐܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܘܿܠܐ ܕܢܬܝܕܥ ܡܢܗ. ܐܝܟ ܡܪ̈ܒܝܢܝܬܐ ܕܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܡܠܠܐ ܗܼܝ. ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܫܟܚ ܝܠܘܕܐ ܕܢܫܡܥ ܡܢܨܪ̈ܐ ܠܗ ܀
33 Indeed, until one arrives at perceiving a being as it is, one applies oneself to knowing it as one wants to perceive it; it does not follow that to know a being endowed with sensitivity, as it is or as it should be known, it suffices to perceive it, just like a nurse who babbles, not as she speaks herself, but as the newborn can understand.

### True motive of the invitation
ܬܪ̈ܥܝܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܝܗܘܕܝܬܐ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܣܒܖܪܐ ܕܡܢܝܚܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܐܦ ܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ ܘܐܦ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܟܕ ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܐܘ ܡܠܦܢܝܐ ܝܗܘܕܝܐ. ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܚܙܝܬ ܘܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܣܥܪܿܬ. ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܐܬܡܠܠ ܘܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܫܡܥܬ. ܐܢ ܚܕ̈ܬܬܐ ܚܙܝܬܝܗܝ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܕ݁ܣܳܥܰܪ ܆ ܡܕܡ ܚܕܬ ܒܥܝ ܡܢܗ. ܡܢܐ ܩܖܿܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܫܖܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܕܟܠܢܫ. ܐܪܐ ܚܝ̈ܠܘܗܝ ܘܬܕܡܪ̈ܬܗ ܠܗܕܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܬܠܡܕܘܟ ܕܬܩܪܝܘܗܝ ܠܫܪܘܬܐ. ܘܬܙܡܢܝܘܗܝ ܕܝܠܥܣ ܒܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ. ܡܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܩܪܝܬ ܥܡܗ ܬܕܡܪ̈ܬܐ ܣܥܪܘ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܬ. ܡܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܙܡܢܬ. ܘܡܢܐ ܐܘܬܪܘܟ ܥܒܳܕ̈ܘܗܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܚܙܝܬ ܕܪܘܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܕܟܠ
34 Now the accounts of the Jews esteemed that one makes God favorable through a feast: thus the Jews formerly, and the Pharisee still, when he invites Jesus or a Jewish doctor. You see one thing and you do another. One says one thing and you hear another. If you have seen Jesus accomplish new things, ask Him for something new. Why do you invite Him to dinner: it is (treating Him) like everyone else. Is it His miracles and His wonders only that gave you the idea to invite Him to dinner and to invite Him to eat at your house? Those whom you invite with Him have therefore done wonders before you? But if you invite those as (ordinary) men, Him also, you invite Him therefore as a man. In what have they enriched you more, His works that you (say) to have seen and to be greater than those of anyone else?
ܐܢܫ ܀ ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܗ ܡܛܠ ܥܒܿܕ̈ܘܗܝ ܝܩܪܬ. ܘܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܩܪܝܬ ܥܡܗ ܡܛܘܠ ܚܘܒܐ ܐܢܫܝܐ ܆ ܒܥܝ ܡܕܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܚܡ ܠܥܒܿܕܵܘܗܝ. ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ
35 If on the contrary you honor Him, Him, because of His works, and, those whom you have invited with Him by a (simple) human love, ask Him therefore what


[p. 482] {#po.41.p482}


[p. 484] {#po.41.p484}
ܕܫܐܠܬ ܡܢܗ ܐܦ ܙܢܝܬܐ. ܐܢܬ ܕܙܡܢܬ ܙܡܢܬ ܣܟܠܐܝܬ. ܘܗܿܝ ܕܠܐ ܩܪܝܬ ܩܪܒܬ ܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ. ܩܪܝ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܬܠ ܠܟ ܘܠܘ ܕܬܬܠ ܠܗ. ܩܪܝ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܘܟܠܟ. ܘܠܘ ܕܬܬܩܢ ܠܗ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܕܡܐܟ̈ܠܬܐ. ܗܼܘ ܐܘܟܠ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܝܟ ܡܢܢܐ ܒܡܕܒܪ̈ܐ. ܗܼܘ ܐܣܩ ܠܗܘܢ ܣܠܘܝ ܡܢ ܝܡܐ. ܗܼܘ ܐܬܩܢ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܫܡܝܢܬܐ ܘܕܗܝܢܬܐ. ܗܼܘ ܐܪܥܝ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܒܫܐ ܡܢ ܟܐܦܐ ܘܡܫܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܐܦܐ ܕܛܪ̈ܢܐ. ܒܗܠܝܢ ܗܼܘ ܬܪܣܝ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܝܟ. ܘܠܟ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܡܬܪܣܐ ܘܙܐܢ ܀
is in relation to His works. That is what even the prostitute asks of Him. You who invite, you invite foolishly; and she who did not invite approaches with wisdom. Invite God so that He may give to you and not so that you may give to Him. Invite God so that He may give you to eat, and not so that you may set a table for Him with dishes. It is He who made your fathers eat manna in the desert, He who for them made quails rise from the sea, He who set before them the table of a fertile and fat land. He who made honey flow for them from the stone and oil from the rock. Of that, it is He who fed your fathers, and you, in secret, He feeds and provides for you.
ܠܳܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܬܤܒܪ ܕܡܬܪ̈ܣܝܬ ܠܗ. ܒܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܗܼܘ ܡܬܪܣܐ ܠܟ. ܩܳܪܺܝܬ ܆ ܩܪܝ ܐܝܟ ܚܟܝܡܐ. ܙܡܢܬ ܒܥܝ ܡܢܗ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܠܦܢܐ. ܠܡܢܐ ܡܫܘܝܬ ܠܗ ܥܡ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܩܪ̈ܝܬ ܥܡܗ. ܐܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܪܚܡܐ ܩܪܝܬܝܗܝ ܥܒܕ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ ܪܫܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܟ. ܐܫܝܓ ܕ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܟܦܪ ܨܐܬܗ. ܣܘܟ ܡܫܚܐ ܥܠ ܪܫܗ. ܗܒ ܠܗ ܫܠܡܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܐ. ܚܘܐ ܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܐ ܕܡܠܐ ܚܕܘܬܐ. ܡܢ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܘܬ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܒܢܦܫܐ ܢܫܩ ܘܥܦܩ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܥܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܣܥܪܝܢ ܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܚܡܝܗܘܢ ܀
36 Therefore do not think that you are going to feed Him. It is with that with which He feeds you that you have invited Him: invite Him as a wise man. You have invited Him, ask Him what is proper to the Doctor. Why treat Him as an equal with those whom you invite with Him? If you invite Him as a friend, do for Him what (befits) friends, that for which He Himself reproached you: wash His feet; wipe His dust; pour oil on His head; give Him the kiss of peace of the friend; show a face filled with joy; kiss Him from the overflow of the love of your soul; and embrace (-Him) as friends are accustomed to do with their friends.
ܐܸܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܥܒܕܬ. ܥܠ ܡܢ ܟܝ ܐܝܟ ܡܿܢ ܕܐܢܬ ܠܐ ܡܚܒܬ. ܐܘ ܗܼܘ ܠܐ ܣܢܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ. ܐܢ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܣܢܝܩ. ܘܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܕܥ ܣܢܝܩܐ ܩܪܝܬܝܗܝ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܫܖܪܵܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܘܠܐ ܡܚܒܬ. ܐܬܟܣܣܬ ܕܩܪܝܬܟ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܗܝ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܘܠܘ ܕܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܠܐ ܫܐܠܬܝܗܝ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܗܝ ܡܘܕܥܐ ܕܠܐ ܩܪܝܬܝܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܠܦܢܐ. ܘܐܠܐ ܚܘܐ ܠܝ ܡܢܐ ܫܐܠܬ ܘܐܝܠܦܬ ܡܢܗ ܡܢ ܕܥܠ ܠܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ ܐܝܢܐ ܦܬܓܡܐ ܡܢ ܡ̈ܠܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܥܝܬ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ. ܘܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܢܒܝܐ ܩܪܝܬܝܗܝ. ܗܐ ܗܼܘ ܚܘܫܒܟ ܡܟܣ ܠܟ. ܗܿܘ ܕܐܦ ܡܢܗ ܐܬܩܛܪܓ ܕܩܥܐ ܘܒܓܢ ܒܟ ܕܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ. ܘܐܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܿܝ ܕܩܪܒܬ ܠܗ. ܡܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܩܪܝܬ. ܘܟܒܪ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܩܪܝܬ. ܕܗܼܝ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܚܫܝܒܐ ܩܪܝܬܐ ܐܬܚܙܝܬ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܀
37 If you do not do that, then why? Like someone you do not love? Or else He, does He not have need of that? If He does not have need of it, why did you invite Him to eat, like someone who had need of it? If on the contrary, which is the truth, it is because you did not love Him as you should have, you deserved the reproach that your invitation was for vain glory and not a mark of distinction. But the fact of not having asked Him what is of God, signifies that you did not invite Him as God, nor either as Doctor. Otherwise, show me what you have asked and learned from Him, since He entered your house. What conversation made of spiritual words have you asked through His intermediary? And if it is not as a prophet that you invited Him, here is that it is your thought itself which accuses you, since it reproached itself for having cried and for having complained in you: "This one is not a prophet, otherwise He would know her who approaches Him." For what motive did you therefore invite? Is it that by chance you would not have invited Him? For what was esteemed to be an invitation seemed to be the opposite.

### What one must ask of God
ܗ̇ܿܘܓܝܪ ܕܩܪ̈ܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܫܐܠ ܡܢܗ ܩܕܡܐܝܬ ܐܠܗܝ̈ܬܐ. ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܒܐ ܘܥܘܠܝܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܡܢ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ. ܨܘܠܠܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܡܢ ܪܓܬܐ. ܒܘܛܠܐ ܕܡܣܟܢܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ. ܬܪ̈ܒܝܬܐ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܗܘܢܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܡܪܓܫܢܘܬܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܡܫܬܘܕܥܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܗ ܡܠܝܬ
38 He who invites God asks Him first of all for divine things: the remission of debts, and the rising of the soul far from sins; for the body, the purification of concupiscence; the disappearance of the indigence of the soul, the increase of spiritual science, the vision for the intelligence of the


[p. 486] {#po.41.p486}
ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ. ܕܘܝܪܐ ܕܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܗ ܫܘܚ̈ܠܦܐ. ܠܐ ܡܨܛܠܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܒܬܐ. ܘܡܬܥܠܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܝܫܬܐ. ܣܢܐܬ ܥܠܡܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܬ ܚ̈ܝܐ ܛܘܒ̈ܬܢܐ. ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܗܿ ܛܘܥܝܝ. ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܠܐ ܐܬܐ ܒܬܪ̈ܗ ܠܠܝܐ. ܫܘܚܠܦܐ ܕܡܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܥܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܡܠܟܐ ܥܠܝܗܿ ܢܘܡܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ ܘܕܥܒܘܕܗ ܘܕܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܒܕܘܟܬܗ. ܚܐܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܗܘ ܘܒܥܠܡܐ ܗܘ ܘܠܘܬ ܥܠܡܐ ܦܝܢܐ ܫܠܝܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܦܬܐ ܘܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܐܢ ܕܒܢܦܫܐ ܘܐܢ ܒܦܓܪܐ ܀
perception of spiritual things, the knowledge of His Economy so rich in meanings; the stay in the place where there are no more changes, the stability in the good, the rising far from evil, the hatred of the world, the love of the blessed life, the science without error, the light which is not followed by the night; the passage from bodily things to spiritual things; the vigilance of the soul inaccessible to the reign of the sleep of sin; the knowledge of his soul and of his creator and of each thing in its place; the liberation from everything that comes from the world, which is in the world and which is turned toward the world; the silence of all the impulses and movements of sin, whether in the soul, or in the body.
ܫܘܬܦܘܬܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܝ̈ܠܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܝܪ̈ܬܘܬܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬ ܫܡܝܐ. ܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܒܥ. ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܣܝܡܬ ܒܢ̈ܝܐ. ܡܪܓܫܢܘܬܗ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܒܗ̇ ܒܡܘܗܒܬܐ. ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܥܒܕܘܗܝ ܥܠܬ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܗܿ. ܕܢܬܚܪܪ ܡܢ ܘܝܐ ܕܣܝܡ ܥܠ ܡܟܫ̈ܠܝܐ. ܕܢܗܘܐ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܝܘܬܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܕܢܗܘܐ ܥܡ ܗܿܝ ܕܬܠܡܝܕܐ ܐܦ ܡܬܠܡܕܢܐ ܕܢܫܬܡܗ ܥܒܕܐ ܛܒܐ. ܘܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܢܣܩ ܠܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܐ ܕܢܩܒܠ ܓܠܝܢܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܙܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܕܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܘܠܟܘܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܡܛܠ ܚܘܒܗ. ܕܢܝܡܘܬ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܬܚܙܐ ܘܚܝܐ ܠܚܕ ܕܢܫܬܘܐ ܠܡܩܢܐ ܡܣܟܢܘܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܒܡܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܦ ܐܠܗܐ ܢܚܘܪ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܚ̈ܝܐ. ܕܢܒܥܐ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܘܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܟܬܝܒ ܠܗ ܀ ܗܠܝܢ ܗܟܝܠ ܘܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܐܠ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ. ܗܿܘ ܕܩܪ̈ܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܚܒ ܘܐܦ ܨܒܿܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܡܬܠ. ܡܛܘܠ ܕܗܢܘ ܨܒܝܢܗ. ܕܓܒܝܠܬܗ ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܢ ܬܗܘܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܡܫܠܡܢܐ ܢܕܥ ܘܢܒܥܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܫܡܠܝܢ. ܘܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܝܠܘ̈ܕܐ ܠܬܪ̈ܣܝܬܐ ܡܚܝܠܬܐ ܕܚܠܒܐ ܢܬܪܓܪܓ. ܐܠܐ ܐܦܢ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܐܘ ܫܡܘܥܐ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܕܢܫܐܠ ܡܫܡ̈ܠܝܬܐ. ܐܢܬ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܓܡܝܪ̈ܢ ܒܥܝ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܟ. ܘܐܢ ܗܿܘ ܩܪܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܢܫܬܒܗܪ. ܐܢܬ ܩܪܝ ܕܬܫܬܒܚ ܡܢ ܡܪܟ. ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܗܘܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܕܩܪܝܬܟ ܀
39 The participation in spiritual powers, the inheritance of the kingdom of heaven; the communion of the Holy Spirit of which one is not satiated; the gift of filial adoption; the sensitization of the intelligence to the gift (of grace), so that his actions might be an occasion of glory for the author of this one: so that we might be liberated from the curse brought against the authors of scandals; so that we might be an occasion of enrichment for others; so that he who is a disciple might be at the same time a teacher, so that he might be called "good servant" and that he might thus rise to the rank of sons, so as to receive the revelation of the mysteries of the Spirit; so that he might be a stranger to the world and to everything that is found there, because of His love; so that he might die to everything that is visible and live for the One; so that he might deserve to possess the poverty of Christ, so that, through the death (endured) for God, he might aim, like that which is alive, to obtain the kingdom and justice, according to what has been written for him. Conclusion: helping the poor. 40 Such are the requests and (others) similar that he who has invited God makes to God, the things that God loves and also that He wants to give. For His will is that he whom He modeled might be (established) in what is great and that, like a perfect (man), he might desire and ask for what is perfect, and not claim, like newborns, the lean food of milk. Even if the Pharisee or an (other) listener did not know what to ask in the way of perfection, you, ask your God for what is perfect; and if he, invited to be praised, you, invite to be glorified by your Lord, since He alone saw your invitation.
° ܬܡܢ ܓܝܪ ܡܥܕ ܝܫܘܥ ܥܐܠ ܡܣܬܡܟ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܗܘܿ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܩܪܐ ܘܡܛܠܬܗ ܡܬܩܪܐ. ܩܪ̈ܝ ܡܕܝܢ ܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ ܠܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ. ܘܗܝܡܢ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܝܬܿܒ݂ ܝܫܘܥ ܥܠ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܟ. ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܩ̇ܪܖ̈ܐ ܐܢܬ. ܠܐܠܗܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܡܢܝܚ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܝܕܥܐ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܬܩܪܐ. ܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܓܝܪ ܟܕ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܡܙܡܢ ܗܘܐ. ܡܛܘܠ ܠܐ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܗ ܐܫܬܟܚ ܩܪܐ ܠܒܪܢܫܐ. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܗܠ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܩܢܘܡܗ ܐܬܩܪܿܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܒܡܙܡܢܢܗ ܠܐ ܚܫܒܗ̇ ܠܩܪܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܕܝܢ ܐܢ ܬܩܪ̈ܐ ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܡܛܠܬܗ ܠܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ ܟܕ ܠܘ ܩܢܘܡܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܡܥܠܬ ܠܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ. ܒܝܕ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܩܪܝܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܡܝܩܪ ܠܗ
41 For Jesus is accustomed to enter to sit, where He is invited alone and invited for Himself. Invite therefore the indigent to your house and believe, that, in them, Jesus will sit at your table. For, in inviting men, through them you give pleasure to God: thus indeed it is clear that it is faith which invites. The Pharisee, in inviting God, found himself inviting man, because of his lack of faith.


[p. 488] {#po.41.p488}
ܠܫܪܘܬܟ. ܘܐܟܠ ܒܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܟܦܢ. ܘܫܬܐ ܒܐܝܕܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܨܗܐ. ܘܠܒܫ ܒܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝ. ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܣܘܢ̈ܩܢܝܐ ܕܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ ܡܡܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܗܼܘ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܗ ܫܩ̇ܠ ܡܘܠܝܐ ܕܣܘܢܩܢܗܘܢ. ܟܦܢܬ ܠܡ ܘܝܗܒܬܘܢ ܠܝ ܠܡܐܟܠ. ܘܨܗܝܬ ܘܐܫܩܝܬܘܢܢܝ. ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܘܟܣܝܬܘܢܢܝ ܀
For one saw it above, He was in person invited, but as the one inviting did not have faith, He did not consider the invitation as made to Him. 42 But you, if you invite with faith, for Him, the indigent, since it is not He in person that you make enter into your house, through those whom you have invited in His honor, He values your meal: in them He has eaten, while He is not hungry; through them He has drunk, while He is not thirsty; in them He is clothed, while He is not naked. When you fill the needs of the poor, it is He, who, upon Himself, bears the weight of their needs: I was hungry, and you gave me to eat; I was thirsty and you gave me to drink; I was naked and you
ܡܢܐ ܐܡ̇ܪ ܐܢܬ ܗܼܘ ܥܫܩ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܣ̈ܘܢܩܢܝܟ. ܡܛܠ ܚܘܒܗ ܘܐܢܬ ܠܐ ܡܡܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܣܘ̈ܢܩܢܘܗܝ. ܗܼܘ ܫܩܼܠ ܟܦܢܟ. ܘܐܢܬ ܠܐ ܝܗܒܬ ܠܗ ܠܚܡܗ. ܩܒܠ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܨܗܝܟ ܘܠܐ ܝܗܒܬ ܠܗ ܫܩܝܐ. ܦܪܣܝ ܢܦܫܗ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܟܕ ܡܟܣܝ ܫܘܒܚܐ. ܘܠܐ ܫܕܝܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܠܒܘܫܗ. ܗܼܘ ܫܩܠ ܕܝܠܟ ܒܛܝܒܘ. ܐܢܬ ܠܐ ܝܗܒܬ ܠܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܒܟܐܢܘ. ܟܦܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܘܨܗܝܐ ܘܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܟ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܘܟܠ ܕܝܢ ܘܢܫܩܐ ܘܢܟܣܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܗܝ ܘܫܩܠ ܝܗܒ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܒܗܝܢ ܝܗܘܒܐ. ܘܫܩܠ ܡܢܟ ܣܘܢ̈ܩܢܝܟ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܢܣܘܒܐ ܀
clothed me. 43 What are you saying? He, He was slandered in person because of your needs, because of His love; and you, you would not fill His needs? He, He endured your hunger; and you, you did not give Him His bread? He endured your thirst, and you, you did not give Him His drink? He showed Himself in person before you, but by hiding His glory, and you did not cover Him with His mantle. He carried what is yours by grace; and you, in justice, you did not give Him what is His. For hunger, thirst and nakedness are of you; giving to eat, to drink and clothing, it is of Him. He brought, He gave you of what is His, so that you might become the donor. And He took away from you your needs, to be, He, the beneficiary.
ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ ܒܥܐ ܕܢܟܚܕܟ ܗܿܘ ܡܪܐ ܕܓܘܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܚܘܣ ܥܠ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ. ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܗܼܘ ܒܥܼܐ ܘܐܚܪܬܐ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܐܝܟ ܕܠܟ ܫܐܠ. ܐܢܬ ܓܝܪ ܐܟܠܬ ܘܫܬܝܬ. ܘܡܬܟܣܝܬ ܒܐܚܪܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܫܘܬܦܟ ܗܼܘ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣܘ̈ܢܩܢܐ ܡܬܡܠܝܢ ܠܗ. ܘܐܢ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܬܐ ܘܐܢ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܐ. ܚܝܒ ܐܢܬ ܕܡܛܝܒܐܝܬ ܬܫܬܡܥ ܘܬܬܠ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ. ܚܘܒܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܛܠ ܕܡܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ. ܘܚܘܒܐ ܡܛܠ ܫܘܬܦܘܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܀ ܟܣܐ ܢܦܫܟ ܐܘܓܒܪܐ. ܡܢܐ ܫܠܝܚܬ ܒܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܐ ܠܟ. ܐܟܘܠ ܡܛܠ ܕܟܦܢܬ. ܐܫܬܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܨܗܝܬ. ܘܐܢ ܬܐܡܪ ܕܐܢܐ ܒܣܒܥܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܘܒܫܩܝܐ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ. ܚܝܘܬܢܘܬܐ ܣܒܥܬ ܒܟ ܘܠܘ ܐܢܫܝܘܬܐ. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܟܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܪ̈ܓܫܝܘܬܐ ܕܣܢܝܩܘܬܗ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܐܝܟ ܚܝܘܬܐ ܐܟܠ ܘܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ. ܗܕܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܐܟܠܢ ܘܠܐ ܢܘܟܠܢ ܕܚܝ̈ܘܬܐ ܗܝ. ܘܟܒܪ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܘܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܣܥܪ ܒܗܝܢ. ܗܒ ܗܟܝܠ ܠܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ. ܐܘܟܠ ܠܟ̈ܦܢܐ. ܐܠܒܫ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ. ܣܥܘܪ̈ ܠܟܪ̈ܝܗܐ. ܕܬܫܡܥ ܩܠܗ ܡܠܐ ܛܘܒܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܝܐ. ܕܬܐ ܒܪܝܟܗ ܕܐܒܝ ܐܝܪܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܡܛܝܒܐ ܠܟ. ܕܠܗ ܡܢ ܝܗܘ̈ܒܐ ܘܢܣ̈ܘܒܐ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܒܟܠܙܒܢ ܠܥܠܡܝܢ ܐܡܝܢ ܀ ܫܠܡ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܥܠ ܚܛܝܬܐ.
44 There are two reasons for which He wanted to make you blush, this interior Master, so that you do not skimp on the gift. The first, because it is He who asked; the second because He claims as if it were for you. It is you indeed who are hungry, who are thirsty and who are clothed in another since he participates in your nature, he whose these needs are already filled. And, whether by debt, or by love, you must obey with eagerness, and make a gift: by debt, because He is the Lord; by love, because of the participation of nature. 45 Clothe yourself, O man! What! You are stripped at the same time as another, and you do not see it? Eat because you are hungry; drink because you are thirsty; but if you say: "Me, I am satiated, I have had quite enough to drink", it is the animality which is satiated in you, and not the humanity. For he who eats without feeling the hunger of another, eats like an animal and not like a man; for eating (oneself), without giving to eat (to others), it is the property of animals and even, perhaps not even, because nature, it too, does that in them. Give therefore to the indigent; make the hungry eat; clothe those who are naked; visit the sick; so as to hear the voice full of goodness of Christ: "Come, blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom which has been prepared for you." To Him, from the part of the donors and the beneficiaries, glory at all times and in the centuries! Amen!

### END OF THE SECOND HOMILY ON THE SINFUL WOMAN


[p. 490] {#po.41.p490}


[p. 492] {#po.41.p492}

### 3rd HOMILY ON THE SINFUL WOMAN
ܬܘܒ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܥܝܢܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܡܬܒܩܝܢ ܒܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ. ܗܢܘܢ ܡܨܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܚܙܝܝ̈ܗܝܢ ܫܪܝܪ̈ܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠ ܚܙܝܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܚܡ ܠܗ ܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܚܙܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܚܝܢܢ ܠܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܕܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ. ܘܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܢܦܫܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܝܢ ܫܘܬܦܘܬܐ. ܒܥܝܢܐ ܚܠܝܡܬܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܗ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ. ܕܠܗܿ ܠܗܕܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܕܢܣܒ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܟܠ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒܥܐ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܢܚܙܐ. ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܘܩܢ ܕܡܠܦܐ. ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܕܟܝܢ
1 Those who, with the eye of faith, consider hidden things, can come to see them in reality, because every man who sees can see what is adapted to him: what is related to the body is seen by the vision of the eyes of the body, and what participates in the soul is seen by the healthy eye of faith which is in his soul. Such is the vision that must acquire whoever seeks to see God, following the very word of our Savior who teaches: Those who have a pure heart can see God.
ܒܠܒܗܘܢ. ܗܢܘܢ ܡܨܝܢ ܕܢܚܙܘܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܀ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܚܝܕ ܒܢܦܫܗ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܫܪ̈ܪܐ. ܘܛܪܕ ܡܢܗ ܦܘ̈ܠܓܐ ܘܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܡܫܬܠܦܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܣܩܘܒ̈ܠܝܗ̇ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ. ܗܼܘ ܡܨܐ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ ܢܬܒܩܐ. ܘܢܣܒ ܡܢܗ ܐܦ ܡܘܗ̈ܒܬܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚܝܢ ܣܝ̈ܓܐ ܕܥܘܘ̈ܟܐ ܕܣܝܡܝܢ ܒܡܨܥܬܐ. ܕܢܟܠܘܢ ܚܪ̈ܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܚܙܝܗ. ܕܢܥܒܪ ܒܗܘܢ ܘܢܬܒܩܐ ܒܡܕܡ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܠܗܠ
2 Indeed, the man who holds the true faith in his soul and drives out from himself the doubts and shifting thoughts that are in opposition to faith can contemplate God and still receive gifts from Him, because there are no obstacles placed across the way to prevent the clarity of his vision from passing beyond them and contemplating what is beyond.
ܡܢܗܘܢ ܀ ܕܠܗ ܠܗܢܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܟܕ ܫܩܠܬ ܥܠܝܗܿ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܐܫܟܚܬ ܕܠܗܿܘ ܕܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܬܗܝܡܢܝܘܗܝ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܬܬܩܪܒ ܠܘܬܗ ܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܘܬ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܘܬ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܫܒܘܩ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܀
3 The sinful woman, carrying within her this vision of faith, obtained the belief that He whom she saw (as) a man was God from God, and (also) to approach Him, not as one does a man, but as one does Him who can forgive sins.
ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܫܡܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܠ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܡܘܠܕܢ ܦܘܠܥܐ ܒܓܘ ܢܦܫܐ ܨܘܚܝ̈ܬܐ ܘܓܘ̈ܕܦܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܡܬܡܠܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܟܕ ܒܙܒܢ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ. ܘܒܙܒܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܒܛܠܢܐ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ ܡܫܡܗܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ. ܘܣܩܘܒܠܗ ܕܡܘܫܐ ܐܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܘܬܘܒ ܕܫܪܐ ܘܐܦ ܡܫܠܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܩܫܝ̈ܫܝܗܘܢ ܘܕܟܗ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܘܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܟܘܠܐ ܘܫܬܐ ܚܡܪܐ. ܘܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܕܡܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܕܚܛ̈ܝܐ. ܥܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܦ ܫܡܪܝܐ ܘܕܝܘܢܐ. ܘܕܒܒܥܠܙܒܘܒ ܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܕܕܝ̈ܘܐ ܡܦܩ ܕܝ̈ܘܐ ܀
4 She had, in fact, heard many things about Christ that had given rise to doubt in (her) soul, those slanders and blasphemies uttered against Him by the Jews: at times they called Him the enemy of God, at other times others labeled Him a destroyer of the law and said that He was the adversary of Moses, and furthermore, that He had even overturned the tradition of their elders and priests || is a (gluttonous) eater, a wine-drinker, a friend of tax collectors and sinners; besides this even, a Samaritan and demon-possessed, and it is by Beelzebub, the prince of demons, that He casts out demons.
ܗܠܝܢ ܘܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܨܥܪ̈ܐ ܟܕ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܥܠ ܝܫܘܥ ܫܡܥܐ ܗܘܬ. ܗܼܝ ܐܝܟ ܬܫܒ̈ܘܚܬܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܩܒܠܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܡ̈ܠܐ ܒܝܫ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܡܬܐܡܪ̈ܢ ܒܓܘ ܢܦܫܗܿ. ܠܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܛܒ̈ܐ ܡܬܗܦܟܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ. ܕܝܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܨܥܪ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܚܣܡܐ ܡܬܝܠܕܝܢ ܘܠܘ ܡܢ ܟܐܢܘܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܡܥܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܥܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܚܫܐ ܢܬܬܙܝܥܘܢ ܠܨܥܪܐ. ܡܓܕܦܝܐ ܠܡ ܐܝܬܝܟ. ܕܟܕ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܥܒܕ ܐܢܬ ܢܦܫܟ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܕܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܢܥܒܕ ܢܦܫܗ ܐܠܗܐ. ܡܥܠܝܐ ܗܝ ܡܢ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܐ
5 These accusations and others of the same kind, the sinful woman, having heard them said of Jesus, had taken for praises; and the malicious words spoken by His enemies were changed in her soul into benevolent thoughts. For she judged that these accusations were born of jealousy and not of justice, because they exceeded the measure of what is ordinarily turned into an accusation by passion. 'You are a blasphemer, because, while you are a man, you make yourself God.' Now, to make oneself God (when one is) a man, that exceeds the measure of human nature.
ܐܢܫܝܐ ܀ ܫܡܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܓܝܪ ܡܕܡ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܡܬܐܡܪ. ܘܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܡܕܡ ܕܡܢܗ ܡܣܬܥܪ. ܘܠܡ̈ܠܐ ܘܠܥ̈ܒܕܐ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܡܦܚܡܐ ܗܘܬ. ܠܗܿܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܘܥܒܕ ܐܢܬ ܢܦܫܟ ܐܠܗܐ. ܗܝ ܡܦܚܡܐ ܗܘܬ ܥ̈ܒܕܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܡܫܠܛܐܝܬ ܡܬܥܒܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܐܢ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܬܐ. ܐܢ ܕܘܟܝܐ ܕܓܪ̈ܒܐ. ܐܢ ܦܘܬܚܐ ܕܣܡ̈ܝܐ. ܐܢ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܕܢܓܝܪ̈ܐ. ܐܢ ܚܘܠܡܢܐ ܕܦܫܝ̈ܓܐ ܀
6 For (if) she had heard what was said about Him, she had (on the other hand) also seen what was accomplished by Him; words and actions, she had compared them one with the other. To the (word): 'You are a man and you make yourself God,' she compared the actions accomplished by Him with authority: whether it be the resurrection of the dead or the purification of the lepers or the (eyes) opened to the blind or the walking of the paralyzed or the health (restored) to the withered-handed.


[p. 494] {#po.41.p494}
ܝܡܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܬܬܩ ܒܩܠܐ. ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܟܕ ܫܠܝܐ ܒܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܠܚܡܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܟܕ ܣܓܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܡܙܐ. ܡܝ̈ܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܬܚܠܦܝܢ ܠܚܡܪ̈ܐ ܒܨܒܝܢܗ. ܐܫܬܐ ܕܥܪ̈ܩܐ ܒܦܫܛ ܐܝܕܗ. ܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܕܡܬܪ̈ܕܦܝܢ ܒܡܠܬܗ. ܠܓܝܘܢ ܟܕ ܩܥܐ ܘܡܒܓܢ. ܘܥܒܝܕ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܗ ܟܣܝܬܐ. ܢܦܫܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܚܣܝܐ ܡܢ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ. ܘܥܒܝܕ ܡܫܪ̈ܪܢܐ ܕܫܘܒܩܢܗܿ. ܚܘܠܡܢܐ ܕܗܕܡ̈ܝ ܦܓܪܗ. ܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܬܣܝܡܝܢ ܡܢܗ. ܝܘܠܦܝܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܟܕ ܡܣܪܚ
7 The sea calmed by a word, the wind appeased by a command, a few loaves multiplied by a sign, water changed into wine at His will, fever chased away by His outstretched hand, demons expelled by His word, Legion screaming and roaring become the mouthpiece of His hidden divinity, a soul purified of (its) sins and assured of (having) its forgiveness, the healing of members of her body, spiritual orders imposed by Him, the teaching of justice proposed to men.
ܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܀ ܠܣܪ̈ܝܚܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܠܬܩܢܘܬܐ. ܠܙܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܟܕ ܡܠܦ ܡܛܟܣܘܬܐ. ܠܙܢܝ̈ܬܐ ܟܕ ܡܩܕܫ ܘܡܠܦ ܟܢܝܟܘܬܐ. ܠܡܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܚܛ̈ܘܦܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܘܥܒܕ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܝܗܘ̈ܒܐ. ܠܕ̈ܓܝܙܐ ܟܕ ܢܬܦ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܫܝܢܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܚ̈ܕܕܐ. ܠܟܠ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܩܪܒ ܆ ܟܕ ܓܕ ܘܡܩܪܒ ܠܗ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ
8 To bring the depraved back to an orderly life, to teach the debauched a disciplined life, to purify prostitutes by teaching them an honest life, to change tax collectors and thieves by suddenly making them generous, to lead the hot-tempered to remain at peace with one another, (in short) to attract whoever approached Him to bring him closer to God His Father.
ܐܒܘܗܝ ܀ ܘܗܘ ܕܡܬܩܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܒܛܠܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ. ܘܡܠܦܢܗܿ ܕܡܛܟܣܘܬܐ. ܘܗܿܘ ܕܡܫܬܡܗ ܗܘܐ ܪܚܡ ܡܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܚܛ̈ܝܐ. ܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܠܗܘܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܝܘܠܦܢܗ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܥܒܕ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܐܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܣܩܘܒܠܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ. ܩܥܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܬܝܬ ܕܐܥܒܕ ܨܒܝܢܝ ܐܠܐ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܫܕܪܢܝ. ܘܕܠܐ ܐܬܝܬ ܕܐܫܪܐ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܘܢܒ̈ܝܐ ܐܠܐ
9 And the one who was treated by the Jews as a drunkard, she saw Him abolishing drunkenness and teaching a disciplined life. The one who had been nicknamed the friend of tax collectors and sinners, she saw Him by His teaching transforming them into friends of God; and the one who was said to be the adversary of God and the enemy of the Law, proclaimed: 'I have not come to do my will, but the will of Him who sent me,' and 'I have not come to destroy the Law and the prophets, but to fulfill (them).'
ܕܐܡܠܐ ܀ ܘܠܗܿܘ ܕܥܫܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܒܒܥܠܙܒܘܒ ܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܕܕܝ̈ܘܐ ܡܦܩ ܕܝ̈ܘܐ ܕܒܟܐܬܗ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܝܬ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܐܦ ܟܕ ܫܬܝܩ. ܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܡܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܪܕܦ ܗܘܐ. ܘܠܗܿܘ ܕܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܓܕܦܢܝܐ. ܥܠ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܫܬܘܕܐ ܗܘܐ ܫܘܒܩܢ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܠܐܢܫܐ. ܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܡܟܣ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܝܗܘܢ ܕܦܠܝ̈ܓܐ. ܘܟܕ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܫܐܠܝܢ ܠܗ ܝܗܒ ܗܘܐ ܀
10 The one whom they accused of casting out demons by Beelzebub, prince of demons, it is He who, only by His rebuke, sometimes even by His silence, cast demons out of someone. The one whom they called a blasphemer, because He promised the forgiveness of sins to someone, she saw Him reproaching them for their thoughts (full) of duplicity and then giving forgiveness to those who asked Him for it.
ܗܠܝܢ ܗܟܝܠ ܟܕ ܚܙܬ ܘܫܡܥܬ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܥܠ ܝܫܘܥ. ܕܢܬ ܒܢܦܫܗܿ ܡܢ ܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܘܐܝܟ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܕ̈ܓܠܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܠܒܝܬܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܬܚܙܐ ܒܗ ܙܡܝܢܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܡܣܬܪ̈ܗܒܐ ܗܘܬ
11 Having therefore seen and heard these things about Jesus, the sinful woman, in herself, judged from His actions that He was God. It was therefore as to God that she ran, carried away by (her) faith. She hastened toward a house


[p. 496] {#po.41.p496}
ܘܟܕ ܥܠܬ ܘܚܙܬܗ ܟܕ ܣܡܝܟ ܆ ܠܐ ܚܪܬ ܒܣܡܟܗ. ܐܠܐ ܒܥܠܬܗ ܕܣܡܟܗ. ܘܠܐ ܐܬܬܪ̈ܥܝܬ ܕܠܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܟܠ ܘܫܬܐ. ܐܝܟܢܝܐ ܐܢܐ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܒܝ ܒ̇ܥܝܐ ܐܢܐ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܟܪ̈ܝܗܐ ܚܟܝܡܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܚܙܐ ܠܐܣܝܐ ܕܡܛܪ̈ܛܫܢ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܪ̈ܗܠܐ ܕܟܪ̈ܝܗܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܠܐ ܢܐܕ ܡܢܗ ܐܘ ܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܡܢ ܗܢܐ ܐܣܒ ܚܘܠܡܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܪ̈ܦܐ ܚܙܬܐ. ܗܽܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܚܙܐ ܘܒܥܐ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ ܆ ܗܟܢܝܐ ܘܐܦ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܝܫܘܥ ܣܥܪܲܬ. ܕܠܐ ܚܪܬ ܒܣܘ̈ܢܝܩܢܐ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܒܗܿܘ ܥܕܢ ܡܫܬܡܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܟܕ ܡܓܣ ܘܐܟܠ ܘܫܬܐ. ܘܣܡܝܟ ܐܝܟ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܙܡܝ̈ܝܐ ܒܝܢܬ ܙܡܝ̈ܢܝܐ. ܐܠܐ ܩܪ̈ܒܬ ܠܘܬ ܗܿܘ ܕܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܐܕܝܩܬ ܒܗ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܥܝܢܐ
of a stranger to see a new guest there. Having entered and having seen Him reclining, she did not pay attention (to the fact that He was taking) His meal, but to the opportunity (given by) His meal; she did not even have this thought: 'To a man who is an eater and a drinker, how shall I ask for the forgiveness of my debts?' 12 But, just as a wise sick person, having seen a physician soil his hands with the infected (wounds) of another sick person, is not upset and does not ask himself how from there he is going to restore his health, but, neglecting what he sees, whatever it may be, implores his skill for healing, in the same way the sinful woman acted toward Jesus: she does not look at the human necessities to which at this hour || He submits, lying down, eating, drinking, being reclined, like one of the guests in the midst of (other) guests, but she approaches the one whom she saw as a man, (but) in whom, with the eye of her faith, she discerned God.
ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܗܿ ܀ ܘܟܕ ܛܥܝܢܐ ܫܛܝܦܬܐ ܕܒܣܡܐ ܩܡܬ ܡܢ ܒܣܬܪܗ ܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܒܟܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܪܚܬ ܕܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܬܗܠܟ ܐܘ ܩܘܕܡܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܒܦܪܗܣܝܐ ܬܩܘܡ. ܐܠܐ ܒܣܬܪ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܒܕܚܠܬܐ ܩܝܡܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܟܕ ܫܪܝܬ ܕܬܒܟܐ. ܒܕܡ̈ܥܝܗ̇ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܡܨܒܥܐ ܗܘܬ. ܐܬܪ̈ܥܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܫܘܝܢ ܠܫܝܓܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ. ܐܠܐ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܗ̇
13 Carrying a jar of perfume, she stands behind Him at His feet, weeping. For, to walk in front of Him, she did not dare, nor to stand before His feet with confidence, but she stood behind His feet in fear. And, having begun to weep, with her tears she flooded His feet. She thought, in fact, that there is no other water for her worthy of washing His feet, if not that of the tears which fell from her head.
ܢܚܬܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܀ ܓܘܡ̈ܨܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܥܝ̈ܢܝܗܿ ܗܿܢܟܘܢ ܕܠܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܛܒܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܚܢܩܝܢ. ܟܕ ܬܪ̈ܥܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܗ̇ ܕܠܝܘ ܡܢ ܛܘܒܥܐ ܘܫܘܙܒܘ ܡܢ ܐܒܕܢܐ. ܘܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܗܿ ܠܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܫܝܓܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܩܕܡܓܝܪ ܕܢܣܚܝܗܿ ܗܼܘ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܥܡܘܕܝܬܐ ܐܣܚܝܬ ܗܝ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܒܡ̈ܝܐ ܚܡܝ̈ܡܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܥܝܗܿ. ܘܚܠܦ ܣܕܘܢܐ ܕܛܥܢ ܗܼܘ ܒܡܟܝܟܘܬܗ ܗܝ ܣܥܪܗ̇ ܣܕܘܢܐ ܥܒܕܬ ܠܗ. ܘܚܠܦ ܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܠܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܕܝܠܗ ܡܟܦܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܀ ܘܕܠܐ ܡܡܪ̈ܚܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܢܘܫܩ̈ܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܝܫܗ ܬܩܪܒ. ܠܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܡܢܫܩܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܡܫܝܚܐ ܒܣܡܐ. ܘܟܕ ܠܙܡܝ̈ܝܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܦܬܘܪܿܐ ܕܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܣܝܡ ܥܒܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܒܘܣܡܗܘܢ ܆ ܦܬܘܪܿܗ̇ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܝܫܘܥ ܥܒܝܕܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ. ܘܡܢܗܝܢ ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܚܫܐ ܒܘܣܡܐ ܡܬܩܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܠܢܘܫܩ̈ܬܐ ܒܢܘܫܩ̈ܬܐ ܡܚܠܦ ܗܘܬ ܠܣܦܘ̈ܬܗ̇ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܬܛܡܐܐ ܡܢ ܢܘܫ̈ܩܬܐ ܕܙܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܒܢܘܫ̈ܩܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܩܕܫܐ
14 For the abysses of her eyes, those very ones that made others sink and tortured them, when she opened access to them, made Him rise from the depths and freed her from perdition. And, with the water from her head, she washed the feet of Christ. For, before He had washed her in the waters of baptism, she washed His feet with the burning water of her tears. And, instead of the towel that He will gird Himself with in His humility, she, with her hair, makes a towel for Him. And, instead of the feet of the disciples that He (would wipe), she wiped His feet. 15 Not daring, with her kisses, to touch His head, with love she kissed His feet and anointed them with perfume. While, for all the guests, the table set before them provided their delights, the feet of Jesus had become the table of the sinful woman, of which, through faith and contrition, she aspired to the delights. She also changed her kisses into (other) kisses: for, her lips, soiled by the kisses of the debauched, she sanctified them by the kisses (given) to the feet of Jesus.
ܗܘܬ ܀ ܘܠܣܥܪܗ̇ ܓܕܝܠܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܦܟܪ ܗܘܐ ܩܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܡܟܠܐ ܠܘܬܗ̇ ܥܒܕܬܗ ܢܫܒܐ ܠܨܝܕܗܿ ܘܒܗ ܐܬܦܟܪܬ ܕܐܬܟܠܝܬ ܩܡܬ ܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ
16 Her braided hair which had seduced the debauched and (had) held them near her, she had made into a personal attraction, but she was embarrassed by it to the


[p. 498] {#po.41.p498}
ܕܡܫܝܚܐ. ܘܠܡܫܚܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܣܡܐ ܕܦܓܪܿܗ̇ ܫܚܠܦܬ ܘܒܡܨܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܓܠܐ ܩܪܒܬܗ ܠܘܬ ܢܦܫܗ̇ ܕܬܬܒܣܡ ܒܗ ܀ ܝܬܝܪ ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܓܠܝ̈ܬܐ ܚܡܝܡܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܘܪ̈ܬܚܢ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܗ̇ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܣܓܝ̈ܐܢ ܗܘܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܡ̈ܥܝܗ̇ ܘܝܬܝܪ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܚܫܐ ܕܒܢܦܫܗܿ. ܬܟܒܢ ܗ̈ܘܝ ܢܘܫܩ̈ܬܗܿ. ܘܝܬܝܪ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܪ̈ܦܬܝ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܒܗܘܢܗܿ. ܒܣܝܡ ܗܘܐ ܪܝܚܗ ܕܡܫܚܗܿ. ܘܝܬܝܪ ܡܢܝܗ ܬܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܚܘܫܒܗܿ ܥܛܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܬ. ܗܿܝ ܕܠܝܫܘܥ ܝܬܝܪ̈ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܗܪ̈ܘܡܝܢ ܡܒܣܡܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܦ ܡܓܪ ܗܘܐ ܪܘܚܗ ܕܒܣܝܡ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܪܝܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܢܦܫܗ̇ ܥܛܪ ܗܘܐ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܡܙܡܢܢܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܦܣ ܒܢܝܫܐ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܩܪ̈ܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܕܠܡܢܘ ܩܪܐ. ܘܟܕ ܠܐ ܪܓܝܫ ܒܚܫܗ̇ ܘܦܘܪܫܢܗܿ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܆ ܫܪܝ ܕܢܬܥܕܠ ܠܘ ܒܗܿ ܐܠܐ ܒܗܿܘ ܕܩܒܠܗܿ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܒܥܕܠܝܗ ܚܒܫ ܗܘܐ. ܟܕ ܠܗܿܝ ܒܚܛܝܬܗ̇ ܡܨܚܐ ܗܘܐ ܘܠܝܫܘܥ ܒܠܐ ܐܝܕܥܬܐ ܥܫܩ ܗܘܐ. ܠܗܿ ܩܪܝ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܘܠܝܫܘܥ ܫܘܬܦܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܝܐ. ܠܗ̇ ܓܢܒܐ ܘܠܝܫܘܥ ܡܩܒܠ ܬܚܠܘܨܐ. ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܨܡܟ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܢ ܢܝܫܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܒܙܥܘܪ̈ ܡܒܥܕ ܗܘܐ ܀
point of being held at the feet of Christ. This perfume which had been the delight of her body, she transforms it and, through the intermediary of the feet (of Jesus), she makes it touch her own soul so that it may be entirely perfumed by it. 17 But more than all this exterior, what burns and boils under the effect of love is the interior of the sinful woman. For her tears are abundant, and the passion of her soul is even more so; her kisses are uninterrupted, and the outbursts of love in her spirit are even more so; the scent of her perfume is delicious, and the repentance that exhales from her thoughts is even more so, which, for Jesus, was more delicious than all aromatics. That is why He waited still, because for Him the perfume of her soul (hers) was delicious. 18 As for the host, the Pharisee, he was ignorant of the intention and the design of the one he had invited; or rather, it is he who did not even know why he had invited, and as he perceives neither the passion nor the discernment of the sinful woman, he began to accuse her, not her, but Him who welcomed her. That is to say that, both of them together, he locks them in his accusation, reproaching the latter for her sin, and accusing Jesus of His ignorance. Her, he qualifies as a sinner; Jesus, as an accomplice of sinners. Her, as a thief; Jesus, as a profiteer. Both of them together he insults, so far is he - and not by a little! - from their design, the two of them.
ܐܠܘ ܠܡ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܕܡܿܢ ܗܝ ܘܡܐ ܛܒܗ̇ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܩܪܒܬ ܠܗ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܨܒܝܬ ܐܘ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܡܠܠܬ. ܐܡܪܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘܐ. ܘܐܘܕܥܬ ܕܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ ܐܠܐ ܐܠܗܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܒܝܐ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܟ. ܝܕܥ ܕܝܢ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ. ܓܠܝܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ. ܠܐ ܐܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܢܒܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܙܕܝܩܐ. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܠܥܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܡܬܚܙܐ ܗܘܐ. ܡܠܬܟ ܕܝܢ ܐܦܩܬܗ ܡܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܢܒ̈ܝܐ. ܝܕܥܬܗ ܒܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܚܘܝܬܗ ܀
19 'If truly this man were a prophet, || would know who she is and what her reputation as a sinner is, this woman who has approached Him.' Without wanting to, O Pharisee, you have spoken the truth, for you said: 'If it were a prophet,' and you signified that He was not a prophet, but God. For, as you said, this man does not have the property of a prophet; but (since) He knows the thoughts of man, it is therefore evident that He is God. For a man does not know what is in man, even if he is a prophet and even if he is righteous. For this one who appeared to the eyes as a man, your word has made Him leave the rank of prophets, (and) His knowledge has designated Him (as being) in the place of God.
ܡܢܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܕܥ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܡܪܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܡܢܟ ܘܒܟ ܝܗܒ ܠܟ ܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܠܚܘܫ̈ܒܝܟ ܩܪ̈ܐ ܣܗ̈ܕܐ ܥܠ ܩܕܝܡܘܬ ܝܕܥܬܗ. ܠܬܐܪ̈ܬܟ ܫܩܠ ܣܦܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܠܦܐ ܕܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܝܕܥ. ܚܘܫܒܟ ܒܣܪܗ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܝܕܘܥܐ. ܗܼܘ ܢܒܣܪܟ ܟܕ ܡܟܣ ܠܗ ܒܓܘ ܢܦܫܟ ܕܝܕܘܥܐ ܗܘ. ܗܿܘ ܕܐܡܪܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܗܿ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ. ܗܐ ܩܥܐ ܠܟ ܒܡܟܣܢܘܬܗ ܕܝܕܘܥܐ ܗܘ ܕܢܦܫܟ. ܕܢܕܥ ܓܝܪ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ. ܠܘ ܣܓܝ ܪܒܐ ܗܘܬ. ܕܠܗܕܐ ܐܦ ܐܢܬ ܐܦ ܙܡܝܢ̈ܝܟ ܝܕܥܬ ܠܗ̇. ܓܠܐ ܠܟ ܕܝܢ ܘܚܘܝܟ ܗܿܝ ܕܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܡܦܣ ܒܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܢܬ ܕܐܬܚܫܒܬ ܘܗܼܘ ܕܚܙܐ ܀
20 For who is the one who can know thoughts, if not the Lord only? By you and in you, He has testified to you that He is God. He has called your thoughts to testify to His foreknowledge. He has accepted your learned interpretation which proclaims that He knows everything. Your thought despised Him as ignorant; He will accuse you of reproaching Him within yourself for knowing. He of whom you say that He does not know the sinful woman, behold, by His reproaches || He cries out to you that He knows you well yourself. For to know that the sinful woman is a sinner, that is not much: that, you and your guests, you knew well; but He has revealed and demonstrated to you what no man knew about her; if you only supposed it, He saw it.


[p. 500] {#po.41.p500}
ܐܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܡ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ. ܚܘܝܟ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܕܗܿܝ ܕܐܢܬ ܝܕܥܬ ܠܗ̇ ܚܛܝܬܐ. ܗܼܘ ܝܕܥܗܿ ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܙܕܝܩܬܐ. ܐܢܬ ܚܙܝܬܗܿ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܗܘܬ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܚܙܗܿ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܐܢܬ ܚܙܝܬܗ̇ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܚܙܗ̇ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܟܕ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܚܫܘܟܐ. ܚܪ ܒܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܥܒܕܗ̇ ܡܕܡ. ܚܪ ܒܛܡܐܬܐ ܘܥܒܕܗ̇ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ. ܒܡܛܢܦܬܐ ܘܚܘܝܗܿ ܕܟܝܬܐ. ܒܙܢܝܬܐ ܘܗܘܬ ܡܨܠܠܬܐ. ܒܙܠܝܠܬܐ ܘܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܐܬܢܟܦܬ. ܒܣܪܝܥܝܬܐ ܘܐܫܬܚܠܦܬ ܠܬܩܢܘܬܐ. ܒܦܗܝܬܐ ܘܗܘܬ ܡܟܢܫܬܐ. ܒܡܟܐܪܬ ܒܫܡܗܿ. ܘܗܘܬ ܒܪܫܥܬܗ ܝܩܝܪܬ ܒܟܘܢܝܐ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܕܥܬܗ̇ ܗ̇ܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܿ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܝܕܥܗ̇ ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܢܫ ܠܐ ܡܦܣ ܗܘܐ ܒܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܬܗ ܒܬܪ̈ܟܢ ܝܕܥܘܗܿ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܠܘ ܒܙܒܢܝܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ. ܘܠܐ ܒܢܘܓܪ̈ܐ ܐܪ̈ܝܟܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܚܕܐ ܕܚܪ ܒܗܿ. ܫܚܠܦ ܥܒܕܗܿ ܡܕܡ ܚܠܦ ܡܕܡ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܕܡ ܚܠܦ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܀
21 'If He had been a prophet, || would have known that she was a sinner'; (but) He showed you that He is God, because, the one whom you knew as a sinner, He, suddenly, knew as righteous. You, you saw her for what she was; but He saw her for what she was not. You, you saw her as darkness as she was, but He saw her as light, from the darkness that she was. He looked at (her) nothingness, He made something of it. He looked at her soiled, || made her holy; pagan, || showed her purified; prostitute, she became pure; debauched, suddenly she became chaste; depraved, she changed and became orderly; flighty, here she is collected; infamous by her name, suddenly become esteemed in her qualification. 22 For you, you knew of her what everyone knew, but He knew of her what was not known about her, except only those who upon the word (of Christ) knew her afterward. And this, not in a long time and after a long period, but at the very instant when || He looked at her, He changed her from one state to another, that is to say from nothing into something.
ܡܢܐ ܡܩܛܪܓܬ ܢܦܫܟ ܫܡܥܘܢ. ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܬ. ܐܦ ܠܐ ܕܠܡܢܘ ܩܪܝܬ. ܡܢܐ ܡܣܪܩܬ ܠܗ ܠܫܘܒܗܪ̈ܟ. ܚܘܬܪܟ ܗܢܘ ܟܠܗ ܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ. ܕܠܡܠܦܢܐ ܘܢܒܝ̈ܐ ܐܥܠܬ ܠܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ. ܘܦܢܝܬ ܘܐܦ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܡܣܒܪ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܡܫܠܚܬ ܠܗ. ܕܠܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܩܪܝ ܡܢܟ. ܐܪܐ ܘܐܠܘ ܝܕܥܗ̇ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘܐ. ܘܐܢܬ ܡܕܝܢ ܕܩܪܝܬܗ ܒܫܡܐ. ܘܥܕܠܬ ܐܦ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܗ̇ ܢܒܝܐ ܐܢܬ ܀ ܡܕܝܢ ܠܘ ܡܿܢ ܕܝܕܥ ܠܗܿ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ. ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܕܡܫܚܠܦ ܥܒܕ ܠܗ̇ ܙܕܝܩܬܐ ܗܘܝܘ ܢܒܝܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܣܗܕܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܙܘܓܟ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ. ܕܗܿܘ ܕܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܫܒܘܩ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܐܢܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ ܫܡܥܘܢ ܝܕܥܬ ܓܠܝܬܗܿ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܠܘ ܟܠܗܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܫܡܗܿ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܫܩܝܠ ܡܢ ܥ̈ܒܕܝܗܿ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܝܕܥ ܡܿܢ ܗܝ ܘܡܐ ܛܒܗܿ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܿܢ ܗܝ ܘܡܿܢ ܗܘܝܐ. ܐܢܬ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܿܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܝܕܥܬ. ܕܡܿܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܘܝܐ ܠܐ ܐܫܬܘܕܥܬ ܀
23 Why reproach yourself, Simon, for not even knowing why you invited? Why do you let your reputation fall? That was all your prestige among your friends, to have introduced into your home a teacher and a prophet; and you do an about-face, and you even let fall this supposition that the one who was invited by you is not a prophet. Is it that even if He knew her to be a sinner, || would be a prophet? And you then, who invited Him in that capacity and who are going to reproach Him still for not knowing her, are you a prophet? 24 Therefore it is not the one who knows the sinful woman as a sinner who is a prophet, but, the one who transforms her by making her become righteous, that is the one who is a prophet. But the one who speaks the truth, that one is God, as is testified by the word of your peers the Pharisees: 'The one who can forgive sins, alone is God.' You then, Simon, you know of her only the exterior, and even then not totally, but only her name drawn from her actions; but He, He knows who she is and what her reputation is, that is to say what she was and what she has become. You, you knew what she was, but, what she has become, you did not understand.
ܒܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܝܢ ܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܕܩܕܝܡ ܠܡܕܡ. ܩܕܝܡܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܗܘܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܗܿܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ. ܡܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ. ܟܠ ܕܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ ܠܐ ܩܪܒ ܠܘܬܗ ܚܛܝܐ. ܘܚܛܝܬܗ ܐܪܐ ܕܚܛܝܐ ܡܚܣܪܐ ܠܙܕܝܩܘܬܗ ܕܢܒܝܐ. ܡܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܟܠܬܢܐ ܡܢ ܡܚܣܢ ܙܚܝܐ ܠܗ. ܘܐܝܟ ܥܫܝܢܐ ܡܢ ܟܫܐ ܠܗ.
25 But, in His knowledge, if there is one thing that has preceded another, what she has become has preceded what she was. How then? It is not every prophet whom a sinner approaches: will not the sin of the sinner damage the righteousness of the prophet? Therefore, to the extent that (the prophet) is stronger than (the sinner), he will conquer him; and to the extent that (Christ) prevails over her, He will subjugate her (the sinful woman); and to the extent that the defilement of sin multiplies, rendering (the sinful woman) impure, far from the righteous


[p. 502] {#po.41.p502}
ܘܐܝܟ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܛܡܐܘܬܗܿ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܡܢ ܩܕܝܫܘܬܗ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܡܛܢܦܐ ܠܗ̇ ܐܪܐ ܦܪܝܫܝܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܠܢܒ̈ܝܐ ܕܡܬܗܓܝܬ ܒܗܘܢ ܡܣܬܟܠܬ ܀
holiness (of Christ), will you not even recognize, O Pharisee, the prophets of whom you are thinking?
ܠܐ ܫܡܥܢ ܐܕܢ̈ܝܟ ܡܕܡ ܕܪܬܡܢ ܣܦ̈ܘܬܟ. ܠܐ ܫܡܥܬ ܩܠܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܢܐ ܩܥܐ ܒܝܕ ܢܒܝ̈ܘܗܝ. ܕܬܘ ܒܝ̈ܝܐ ܬܝ̈ܒܐ ܘܐܣܐ ܬܝܒܘܬܟܘܢ. ܘܐܬܦܢܘ ܠܘܬܝ ܘܐܬܦܢܐ ܥܠܝܟܘܢ ܐܿܡܪ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܚܝܠܬܢܐ. ܘܐܢ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܚܛ̈ܗܝܟܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܣܢܘܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܬܠܓܐ ܢܬܚܘܪܪܘܢ. ܠܐ ܡܫܬܘܕܥܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܩ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܐ ܥܗܕܬ ܩܪܝܢܟ ܕܢܒ̈ܝܐ“ ܀
26 Have your ears not heard what your lips were murmuring? Have you not heard the voice of God, what He proclaims through His prophets? 'Return, repentant children, and I will heal your penance.' 'Return to me, and I will return to you, says the Lord Almighty.' 'Even if your sins were like scarlet, like snow they will whiten.' Do you not recognize these voices that come from them? Do you not remember your reading of the prophets?
ܐܠܗܐ ܩܪܐ ܘܡܦܝܣ ܘܡܩܪ̈ܒ ܠܘܬܗ ܠܚܛ̈ܝܐ. ܗܿܘ ܕܡܠܦܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܪ̈ܒ ܡܢ ܢܒ̈ܝܐ. ܘܠܝܫܘܥ ܥܠ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܡܛܒܬ ܠܗ ܒܫܘܒܗܪܐ ܕܡܠܬܟ ܕܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ. ܐܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܛܠ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ. ܡܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܥܠ ܕܩܪ̈ܐ ܠܚܛ̈ܝܐ. ܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ. ܐܠܐ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܐܡܪܬ ܆ ܕܢܒ̈ܝܐ ܓܝܪ ܘܙܕܝܩ̈ܐ ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܢܥܪܩܘܢ ܡܢ ܚܛܝܬܐ. ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܩܪܘܒ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ ܘܢܫܒܩܝܗܿ ܘܢܥܒܕ ܗܿܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܢܒܝܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܡܥܒܕ. ܢܒܝܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܘ ܫܒܘܩܐ ܗܘ. ܐܠܐ ܡܩܒܠ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܘܠܘ ܡܙܕܩܢܝܐ ܐܠܐ ܡܙܕܕܩܢܐ. ܘܠܘ ܝܗܒ ܚܘܣܝܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܩܒܠܢܐ ܕܚܘܣܝܐ. ܘܠܘ ܝܕܥ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܒܪܘܚܐ ܚܙܝܐ
27 God has called, by persuading and by making sinners approach Him, He about whom you teach that He is greater than the prophets. Now, to Jesus, because the sinful woman approaches Him, you are going to announce to Him, while glorifying yourself in your word, that He is not a prophet! If Jesus, because the sinful woman has approached Him, is not a prophet, then God is not God either, because He calls sinners. However, you have said with truth that the property of prophets and the righteous, in fact, is to flee from a sinful woman, but the property of God is to approach a sinful woman, to forgive her, and to do what a prophet cannot do. For a prophet does not give forgiveness, but he receives forgiveness; he does not justify, but he is justified; he does not show mercy, but he receives mercy; he does not know what is hidden, but he sees in spirit what is hidden.
ܕܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܢܫܠܐ ܐܢܚܢܢ ܡܢ ܡܟܣܢܘܬܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܘܢܫܡܥ ܡܠܬܐ ܡܟܣܢܝܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܟܬܝܒ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܒܢܦܫܗ ܐܬܚܫܒ ܘܐܣܬܒܪ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܣܬܒܪ. ܩܠܗ ܕܝܫܘܥ ܡܦܪ̈ܣܐ ܠܚܘܫܒܗ. ܘܫܩܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܗ ܘܣܐܡ ܡܕܡ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܡܦܩ ܦܠܝܓܘܬܗ ܠܓܠܝܐ ܕܚܙܝ̈ܘܗܝ. ܘܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܒܝܕ ܢܦܫܗ ܝܿܗܒ ܠܗ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܡܢܗ ܝܕܥ ܠܗ̇ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܫܡܥܘܢ ܠܡ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܡܪ ܠܟ. ܡܥܬܕ ܠܗ ܒܩܠܗ ܠܡܟܣܢܘܬܐ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܫܪ̈ܐ ܠܡܟܣܢܘܬܗ. ܩܪܝܗܝ ܒܫܡܗ. ܘܐܘܕܥܗ ܚܘܫܒܗ. ܫܡܥ ܕܐܿܡܪ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܡܕܡ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܿܡܪ ܠܟ ܀
28 As for us, let us leave aside the reproach of the Pharisee, and let us listen to the word of reprimand from God toward him. For, as for what is written about him, namely that he was thinking in himself and that he was imagining what he was imagining, the voice of Jesus reveals his thought, tears his secret from him, and puts things before him. It is going to bring his duplicity into the light before those who see him, and (Jesus), from his own (words), provides him with the proof that He knows better than he that she is a sinner. 'Simon, I have something to say to you.' By His words || He prepares him for the reprimand and || He begins then His reprimand. He calls him by his name and makes him know his thought. Listen to what He says to him: 'I have something to say to you.'
ܘܢܦܠ ܒܡܣܒܪ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܘܦܘܪ̈ܬܟܐ. ܟܕ ܡܦܝܣ ܣܟܡܬܢܐܝܬ ܕܢܫܡܥ. ܘܡܝܩܪ ܠܗ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܩܪܝܗܝ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܫܐܝܠܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܿܠܡ ܠܗܿ. ܐܡܼܪ ܠܡ ܪ̈ܒܝ. ܘܐܢ ܪܒܟ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܆ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܪܬ ܕܠܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܘ. ܐܘ ܫܿܠܡ ܠܡܠܬܟ ܐܘ ܫܿܠܡ ܠܚܘܫܒܟ. ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܐܦܩܬܝܗܝ ܡܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܢܒ̈ܝܐ. ܠܡܢܐ ܒܓܠܝܐ ܒܡܣܒ ܒܐ̈ܦܐ ܇ ܪ̈ܒܝ ܡܟܪܙܬ ܠܗ
29 And (Simon) throws himself into an anxious wait, while he makes it believed hypocritically that he is listening. And he renders honor to the very one whom he invited, by making Him an incongruous and inconsiderate request. He says: 'My Master.' But if He is your Master, how did you say above: 'He is not a prophet'? Either this agrees with what you say, or it agrees with what you think. For, in secret, you have removed Him from the rank of prophets. Why in public hypocritically do you proclaim Him: 'My Master,' and speak with duplicity to Him who searches all hearts, and testify by accusing yourself


[p. 504] {#po.41.p504}
ܘܡܡܠܠܬ ܒܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܒܝ̈ܢ ܇ ܥܡ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܐܿܫ ܟܠ ܠܒ̈ܝܢ. ܘܣܗܕܬ ܒܡܟܣܢܘܬܟ ܕܠܘܩܒܠ ܢܦܫܟ ܕܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܘܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܡܿܟܣ ܠܟ ܀
is it with justice that He too, in agreement with you, gives you remonstrances?
ܐܠܐ ܘܐܦ ܗܢܐ ܥܝܕܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܗܘ ܝܠܦܝܢ ܘܠܘ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ. ܘܒܡܣܒ ܒܐ̈ܦܐ ܗܘ ܡܬܚܫܚܝܢ. ܘܠܘ ܒܬܪ̈ܝܨܘܬܐ. ܘܒܠܒܐ ܘܒܠܒܐ ܡܡܠܠܝܢ. ܘܠܘ ܒܠܒܐ ܦܫܝܛܐ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܢܫ ܕܒܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܐ ܡܬܙܝܥ. ܕܟܕ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܡܬܚܫܿܒ ܆ ܠܫܡܘ̈ܥܘܗܝ ܡܕܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ ܢܡܠܠ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܠܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܡܡܠܠ ܆ ܠܐ ܡܬܟܣܐ ܡܠܬܗ ܒܐܝܕܥܬܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ. ܘܒܪ ܫܥܬܗ ܐܬܚܓܪ ܠܗ. ܒܿܥܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܗܠܟ ܕܓܠܐܝܬ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܕܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܟܚ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܩܿܒܠ ܫܒܝܠܗܿ ܕܩܘܫܬܐ ܕܢܕܪܘܟ ܒܗ ܚܘܫܒܐ
30 But that is how those who teach that this is skill and not truth ordinarily act. They use hypocrisy and not uprightness; they speak with a double heart and not with a simple heart. Such is the art of every man who is moved by human thoughts: while he thinks one thing, he will say another to his listeners. And as he speaks to men, his words, such as they are known, are not reprehensible: this is what the Pharisee intends to do, but immediately he is prevented: for he wants to walk while lying on the path of truth and he cannot, for one does not take the path of rectitude to trace thoughts of lies there. He says: 'My Master, what you want'
ܟܕܒܐ. ܐܡܪ ܠܡ ܪܒܝ ܡܕܡ ܕܨܒܐ ܐܢܬ ܀ ܐܿܡܖ ܠܗ ܝܫܘܥ ܆ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܒܝܢ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܘ ܠܚܕ ܡܪܐ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܚܕ ܚܝܒ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܕܝܢܪ̈ܐ ܚܡܫܡܐܐ. ܘܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܚܡܫܝܢ. ܘܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܦܪܥ ܆ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܫܒܼܩ. ܐܝܢܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܝܬܝܪ ܢܚܒܝܘܗܝ ܆ ܐܿܡܪ ܫܡܥܘܢ ܗܿܘ ܕܐܫܬܒܩ ܠܗ ܣܓܝ. ܐܟܣ ܢܦܫܗ. ܘܠܐ ܐܪ̈ܓܫ. ܫܪܐ ܠܠܐ ܐܝܕܥܬܗ ܒܡܠܬܗ. ܘܠܐ ܚܫܼܬ ܢܦܫܗ. ܐܡܼܪ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܐܫܬܒܩ ܠܗ ܣܿܓܝ ܆ ܗܼܘ ܘܿܠܐ ܕܢܚܒ ܣܓܝ ܀
31 Jesus says to him: 'A creditor had two debtors; one owed him five hundred denarii, and the other fifty. And as they did not have the means to pay, he forgave both (their debts). Which of them will love him more (the creditor)?' Simon says: 'The one to whom he forgave more.' He accused himself, without noticing it. He gave free rein to his ignorance by his words and he does not suffer from it, he says: 'To the one to whom he forgave more, it is fitting that he love more.'
ܘܚܘܝ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܗ̈ܘܝܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ. ܟܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܥܗܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ̇ ܕܚܘܒܬܐ ܆ ܡܬܕܟܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܕܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܒܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܚܝܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܚܘܒܐ ܠܘܬ ܫܒܘܩܗܿ ܡܣܓܝܐ ܗܘܬ. ܠܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܬܬܥܕܠ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܐܚܒܬ ܣܿܓܝ ܕܣܿܓܝ ܐܫܬܒܩ ܠܗܿ. ܕܡܥ̈ܝܗ̇ ܡܟܪ̈ܙܢ ܥܠ ܚܫܗܿ. ܚܫܗ̇ ܣܿܗܕ ܥܠ ܚܘܒܗܿ. ܚܘܒܗܿ ܢܣܝܒ ܡܢ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܪ̈ܒܘܬ ܫܘܒܩܢܗ̇ ܡܬܝܕܥܐ ܡܢ ܣܓܝܐܘܬܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܝܗܿ. ܣܓܝܐܘܬ ܚܛܝܬܗܿ ܡܟܪܙܐ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܛܒܘܬܗ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܫܿܒܩ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܘܗܝ ܬܪ̈ܝܟܘܢ ܚܝ̈ܒܐ ܐܿܘ ܫܡܥܘܢ. ܗܿܝ ܕܐܪ̈ܓܫܬ ܕܣܓܝܐܐ ܚܘܒܬܗܿ ܆ ܣܓܝܐܝܬ ܫܩܠܬ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܘܐܢܬ ܕܠܐ ܐܪ̈ܓܫܬ ܒܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܬܟ & ܐܝܟ ܟܡܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܓܫܬ ܡܩܒܠܬ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܠܘ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܘܗܝ ܕܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܨܒܼܐ ܡܪ̈ܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܢܠܦܢ. ܐܠܐ ܕܟܕ ܣܓܝܐܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܆ ܠܐ ܡܪܓܫ ܗܘܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܣܦܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܥܒܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܚܿܙܝܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܕܢܗܘܐ ܚܳܙܝܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ. ܕܗܿܝ ܓܝܪ ܟܡܐ ܕܣܓܝ̈ܐܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܚܛܗ̈ܝܗ̇ &܀: ܗܟܢܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܪ̈ܓܫܢܘܬܗ̇ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܘܠܐ ܡܪܦܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܿ ܟܐܒܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ̇ ܕܬܫܒܘܩ
32 Therefore he showed that everything that had been done by the sinful woman had been done justly: for, the more she recalled the greatness of the debt, the more she made mention of the greatness of the forgiveness; and the more she considered the forgiveness, the more she made the love for the one who forgives her grow. Therefore, do not reproach the sinful woman for having loved much, because she has been forgiven much. For her, her tears proclaim her pain, and her pain testifies to her love. Her love comes from the memory of the forgiveness. The greatness of her forgiveness is appreciated according to the great number of her sins. The great number of her sins proclaims the richness of the grace of the one who forgives. 33 You and she, both of you, (are) debtors, Simon. This one, who has the feeling of her superabundant debt, has superabundantly received forgiveness. And you who do not have the feeling of the greatness of your debt, you only receive forgiveness to the extent that you have had the feeling (of it). Our Lord wants here to teach us, not that the sins of the Pharisee are lesser, but that, while they are numerous, he does not have the feeling of them. For it was not enough to make oneself a spectator of the sins of others to see one's own sin. This one, (the sinful woman), in fact, to the extent that her sins were numerous, had a vivid feeling of them and the sickness of her soul did not allow her to forgive herself her sin and to examine that of others.
ܚܛܝܬܗܿ ܘܬܬܒܿܩܐ ܒܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܀ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܣ̈ܗܕܢ ܐܦ̈ܝܗ̇ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܗܿ̇ ܆ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܥܝ̈ܢܝܗ̇ ܒܗܿܘ
34 Just as her face is the witness of her soul, just as her eyes
ܥܕܢ ܒܙܡ̈ܝܝܐ ܠܐ ܚܝܪ̈ܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܆ ܘܐܦ ܠܐ ܒܡܕܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܬܒܩܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܒܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܡܪ̈ܢ ܆ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ̇ ܒܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܚܐܪ ܗܘܐ. ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܒܥܘܠܐ ܕܩܝܘܡܗܿ. ܒܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܡܕܡ ܕܚܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܗ ܡܪ̈ܓܫܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܕܗ̇ܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܪ̈ܓܫܐ ܗܘܬ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܡܩܒܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܝܠܗ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܥܠ ܕܐܪ̈ܓܫܬ ܒܣܓܝ ܚܛ̈ܗܝܗ̇ ܆ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܝܗ̇ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܡܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܫܩܠܬ ܗܘܬ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܝܗܿ ܒܡܢܝܢܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܢܬܟܢܘܢ ܀
at that moment were not fixed upon the hosts and that she considered nothing else but the feet of Our Lord, just so she did not fix her own gaze upon the sins of others, but only upon her personal iniquity. For that upon which she focused her attention, she perceived, and, what she perceived, she obtained forgiveness for. Therefore, because she had felt her many sins, she had obtained from Jesus the forgiveness of her many sins, so that the forgiveness of her sins was declared to be credited to the right hand.
ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܡܘܪ̈ܒ ܘܡܣܓܐ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܝܗ̇ ܆ ܣܡܗ ܒܡܠܬܗ ܒܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ. ܘܟܕ ܡܣܬܒܪ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܥܠ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܝܐ ܣܐܡ ܆ ܛܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܘܐܩܝܡܗ ܠܘܬ ܐܝܕܐ ܕܣܡܠܐ. ܘܒܫܘܚܠܦ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܐܦ ܫܚܠܦ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܛ̈ܗܐ. ܦܠܓ ܘܝܗܒ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܕܘܟ̈ܝܬܐ. ܠܘܬ ܗ̇ܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܣܓܝܐܝܬ ܝܕܥܬ ܚܛ̈ܗܝܗܿ ܐܠܗܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܐܝܬ ܝܕܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܠܘܬ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܕܡ ܙܥܘܪ̈ ܐܪ̈ܓܫ ܒܚܛܝܬܗ ܆ ܐܠܗܐ ܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܘܥܫܝܢܬܐ ܝܕܥܗܿ ܀
35 For, while Jesus increases and multiplies the number of her sins, He places (this one) by his words on the right side; and while one thinks that he credits the Pharisee with only a few sins, He rejects him and places him at his left hand: by changing the account of the sins, He also changes the forgiveness of the sins. He decides, and gives to both (debtors) two places: for her, who has an overabundant knowledge of her sins, God keeps only a restricted knowledge; but for the other, who has only a restricted feeling of his sin, God knows this one to be great and considerable.
ܘܐܟܣܗ ܕܡܛܠ ܕܐܣܒܪ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܐ ܣܢܝܩܐ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܐܣܓܝ ܝܩܪ ܐܝܟ ܣܢܝܩܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܠܪ̈ܓܠܐ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܫܝܓ. ܘܠܐ ܡܫܚܝܐ ܥܠ ܪܝܫܐ ܐܪ̈ܡܝ. ܘܠܐ ܢܘܫܩ̈ܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܚܘܝ. ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܩܪܝܬܐ ܫܚܝܡܬܐ ܥܒܕ. ܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܛܠ ܚܙ̈ܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܝܫܘܥ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܦܟܝܗ ܗܕܐ ܚܘܒܗ ܆ ܦܟܝܗ ܗܘܐ ܐܦ ܩܘܒܠܗ. ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܝܩܪ̈ܗ ܒܠܫܢܝܗ ܗܘܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘܠܘ ܒܢܦܫܗ ܆ ܟܕ ܒܣܦܘ̈ܬܗ ܪܒܝ ܩܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܘܒܢܦܫܗ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܚܫܒ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܆ ܗܟܢܐ ܘܐܦ ܩܘܒܠܗ. ܒܩܠܗ ܡܬܕܡܐ ܗܘܐ. ܕܒܫܓܡܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘܠܘ ܒܦܘܪ̈ܫܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܣܒܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܩܪܐ ܆ ܒܢܦܫܗ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܩܪܐ ܘܠܘ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܀
36 And He reproaches him: since he imagines himself not to be needy, for that reason, he will have little regard for his indigence. He did not wash His feet with water; he did not pour perfume on His head; he did not manifest his friendship with kisses; but he only made a banal invitation, that is to say for the sake of the spectators, but not for the sake of Jesus. Since this love of his is vain, vain also was his welcome. Just as his honors were only on his tongue and not in his soul, by calling Him Rabbi with his lips, while in his soul he had only some sort of esteem, so also was his welcome. To hear him, it resembled what one does without attention and not with discernment: outwardly he imagined he was inviting Jesus, but within himself it was his friends he invited, and not Christ.
ܐܬܦܢܝ ܠܡ ܝܫܘܥ ܠܘܬ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܿܝ. ܘܐܡܪ ܠܫܡܥܘܢ. ܪܒܐ ܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ. ܚܘܪ̈ܐ ܠܘܬܗ̇ ܘܡܠܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܐܚܒܬ ܐܬܪܚܡܬ. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐ ܩܪܒܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܫܐܝܠܐܝܬ. ܠܐ ܡܠܠ ܥܡܗ̇ ܫܚܝܡܐܝܬ. ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܕܝܿܢܐ ܕܚܐܪ ܒܡܬܩܛܪ̈ܓܢܐ ܘܡܫܐܠܐ ܠܡܩܛܪ̈ܓܢܗ ܆ ܗܟܢܐ ܚܐܪ ܗܘܐ ܒܚܛܝܬܐ ܘܡܫܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܫܡܥܘܢ. ܚܳܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܡ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܕܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܝܕܥܬ ܠܗ̇ ܠܗܕܐ. ܕܡܣܒܪܬ ܕܝܕܥܬ ܠܗܿ. ܗܕܐ ܕܡܬܩܛܪ̈ܓܐ ܡܢܟ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ. ܗܕܐ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ̇ ܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ ܡܬܪ̈ܫܐ ܐܢܐ. ܘܠܗ̇ ܡܛܠ ܚܛܝܬܗܿ. ܘܠܝ ܡܛܠ
37 Jesus turned toward this woman and spoke to Simon. O great testimony of love! For her, a look; for the other, a word! In the way she loved, mercy was shown to her. Just as she did not approach Him by pretense, (likewise) He did not speak with her in a banal way; but like a judge who fixes the accused and refutes his accuser, thus He fixed the sinner and refuted Simon: Do you see this woman? That is to say, do you know her, she whom you imagine you know? She who is accused by you in secret; she because of whom I too am blamed by you: she, because of her sin, and I, because I welcomed her: you have condemned us both.
ܕܩܒܠܬܗ̇ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܚܘܝܒܐ ܣܐܡ ܐܢܬ ܀ ܡܛܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܫܝܬܢܝ ܒܢܦܫܟ ܕܩܒܠܬܗܿ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ. ܗܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܝ̈ܝܐ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܟܘܢ. ܘܬܩܠ ܐܢܐ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܝܠܟ ܘܕܝܠܗܿ. ܘܒܡܣܐܬܐ ܕܟܢܘܬܐ
38 But since in yourself you have reproached me for having welcomed the sinner, behold, I become the judge of you both, and I am going to weigh your debt


[p. 506] {#po.41.p506}


[p. 508] {#po.41.p508}
ܚܘܒܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܟܘܢ ܣܐܡ ܐܢܐ. ܐܢܬ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܣܒܪܬ ܥܠܝ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܘܕܦܫܝܩܐܝܬ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܡܬܩܪܐ ܐܢܐ. ܩܪܝܬܢܝ ܩܪܝܬܐ ܫܚܝܡܬܐ ܥܡ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܆ ܟܕ ܗܝ ܗܕܐ ܕܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܬܢܝ ܡܠܦܐ ܕܚܫܒܬܢܝ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ. ܐܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܝܩܝܪ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܥܝ̈ܢܝܟ ܘܚܫܒܬܢܝ ܆ ܐܦ ܐܝܟ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܢܐ ܘܕܝ̈ܝܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ. ܠܐ ܡܡܪܚ ܗܘܝܬ ܕܦܫܝܩܐܝܬ ܬܩܪܝܢܝ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܠܐ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܢܫ ܫܚܝܡܐܝܬ ܗܕܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܡܥܒܕ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܪ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܐܝܩܪ̈ܗܘܢ. ܡܕܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܗܕܐ ܕܡܣܒܪܬ ܕܝܩܪܬܢܝ ܐܬܚܙܝܬ ܕܣܓܝ ܨܥܪܬܢܝ. ܕܩܪܝܬܢܝ ܠܒܝܬܟ ܐܝܟ ܚܒܪܐ. ܘܐܥܠܬ ܥܡܝ ܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܐܝܟ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܀
and hers; and on the scale of justice I am going to place the debt of you both. You, because you judged me to be some (man) or other and I was invited in a banal way with everyone, you made me an ordinary invitation with many (others), this fact of inviting me with others making it understood that you esteemed me their equal. For if I had been in honor in your eyes and you had esteemed me, even as one of the notables and judges of (this) world, you would not have dared to invite me so simply, because, even with regard to them, no one can make such an ordinary invitation, except one who enjoys the same measure of consideration as they do. Therefore, in that very thing where you imagined you were honoring me, it seemed that you were doing me great injury, because you were inviting me to your home as an equal, and, with me, as with an ordinary (man), you were introducing your friends.
ܐܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܡܦ̈ܣ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܝ. ܕܡܿܢ ܐܝܬܝ ܆ ܗܺܝ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܠܝ ܩܢܛܪܘܢܐ ܐܦ ܐܢܬ ܐܿܡܪ ܗܘܝܬ ܠܝ. ܘܟܕ ܐܢܐ ܐܚܛܘܦ ܗܘܝܬ ܗܕܐ ܕܐܥܘܠ ܠܒܝܬܟ. ܐܢܬ ܡܢܗ̇ ܐܝܟ ܐܺܝܕܐ ܕܖ̈ܒܐ ܡܢܟ ܡܫܬܐܠ ܗܘܝܬ. ܗܟܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܦ ܩܢܛܪܘܢܐ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬܝ ܿ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܬܥܘܠ ܬܚܝܬ ܡܛܠܠܝ. ܐܠܐ ܐܡܼܪ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܘܢܬܐܣܐ ܛܠܝܝ. ܘܫܡܥܘܢ ܬܠܡܝܕܝ. ܒܥܠ ܫܡܟ. ܗܿܘ ܕܫܡܗ ܠܘܬܟ ܘܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܗ ܓܠܝܙ ܡܢܟ. ܟܕ ܨܒܝܬ ܕܢܩܒܠܢܝ ܒܣܦܝܢܬܗ ܆ ܐܬ ܪܗܒ ܕܦܪܘܩ ܠܟ ܡܢܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܓܒܖܐ ܐܢܐ ܚܿܛܝܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܝܕܥܘܢܝ ܗܟܢܐ ܚܓܘ ܘܐܫܬܐܠܘ ܒܕܚܠܬܐ. ܘܩ̈̇ܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܟܚܝܢ ܕܒܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܢܩܒܠܘܢܢܝ ܀
39 For, if you had known who I was, what the centurion said to me, you would have said it to me too; and while I would have seized this (opportunity) to introduce myself to your home, you, on this unhoped-for occasion for you, would have prayed to me. It is thus that the centurion had said to me: I am not worthy that you should enter under my roof, but say a (single) word and my child will be healed. And Simon, my disciple, your namesake, he whose name is close to yours, but whose discernment is very far from yours, when I wanted him to welcome me into his boat: Depart from me quickly, because I am a sinful man. For those who had this knowledge of me had been afraid and out of fear refused (to receive me): they cried out that they could not receive me into their homes.
ܐܢܬ ܕܝܢ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܦܣܬ ܒܝ ܕܡܿܢ ܐܝܬܝ ܩܪܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܒܫܘܒܗܪܐ. ܘܡܥܠܬ ܠܝ ܠܒܝܬܟ ܟܕ ܚܬܝܪ̈ܬ. ܘܡܦܩܩܬ ܩܕܡ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܆ ܕܩܪܝܬ ܠܒܝܬܝ ܗܿܘ ܕܣܒܝܪ̈ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܒܥܝ̈ܢܝ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܕܡܢ ܗܖ̈ܟܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܐ. ܕܐܠܘ ܠܐ ܐܣܬܒܪܬ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܚܙܝܐ ܆ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܒܝܬܟ ܡܿܙܡܢ ܗܘܝܬ ܠܝ. ܡܢ ܡܣܒܪ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܕܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܥܠܝ ܐܬܢܟܦܬ. ܘܠܘ ܡܢ ܐܝܩܪܐ ܕܠܝ ܫ̇ܘܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܟ ܡܟܣ ܠܟ ܗܢܐ ܕܐܬܩܢܬܝܗܝ ܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܕܐܦ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܩܪ̈ܝܬ ܥܡܝ ܥܒܼܕܬ ܨܐܕܝ. ܕܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܬܬܝܕܥ ܕܡܕܡ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܚܫܝܒ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܀
40 But you, because you do not know who I am, but you invite me out of pride, you make me enter your home, boasting with arrogance before everyone: "I have invited into my home the one who passes for great in the eyes of men", - which, by that very fact, makes it known that if I had not appeared great to the spectators, you would not have invited me into your home either. Therefore it is to the opinion of others regarding me that you are attached, and not to the honor that is worthy of me; and even your table accuses you, the one that you prepared for me as for a man, and that on the other hand again for me you did nothing more than for those whom you invited with me, so that you recognize by that that I was not judged by you more honorable than them.
ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܠܟ ܚܫܝܒܐ ܚܿܛܝܬܐ ܘܠܝ ܪܫܝܬ ܥܠ ܕܩܒܠܬܗ̇ ܆ ܢܛܪܬ ܒܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܐܝܩܪܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܡܪܚܬ ܕܠܒܝܬܗܿ ܬܩܪܝܢܝ. ܡܦܝܣܐ ܗܘܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܗܕܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܗܝ ܡܢܗܿ. ܟܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܚܿܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܆ ܡܬܒܩܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܙܥܘܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܿ. ܘܖ̈ܢܬ ܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܕܠܐ ܣܦܩܐ ܠܗܕܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܬܩܪ̈ܐ ܠܥܘ ܒܝܬܗܿ. ܘܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܪܗܛܬ
41 But this woman, who by you is considered a sinner and whom you reproach me for having received, observed, she, with discernment a whole ceremony of honor by not daring to invite me to her home, for she was persuaded that it was too much for her. For the more she saw my greatness, the more she considered her own smallness. And she thought with wisdom that she was in no way capable of inviting God into her home, and that is why she


[p. 510] {#po.41.p510}
ܘܒܒܝܬܟ ܩܖܒܬ ܠܝ. ܘܟܕ ܝ̈ܕܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܡܬܪ̈ܫܝܐ ܡܢܟ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܐܒܣܪ̈ܬ ܥܠ ܫܝܢܟ ܕܠܘܬܗܿ ܆ ܘܫܛܬ ܐܦ ܒܗܬܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܙܝܐ. ܘܐܩܠܝܬ ܥܠ ܥܕܠܝܐ ܕܙܡܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܒܣܖ̈ܬ ܥܠ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܟܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ̇ ܀ ܘܒܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܐܟܪ̈ܬ ܡܪ̈ܚܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܕܩܢܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܡܢ ܕܐܚܨܦܬ ܘܥܠܬ ܠܘܬܝ ܆ ܩ̇ܪܒܬ ܠܝ ܗܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܿܚܒ ܐܢܐ. ܘܐܬܩܢܬ ܩܕܡܝ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ̇ ܆ ܠܘ ܕܐܢܐ ܐܣܒ ܡܢܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܕܐܣܦܩܝܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܚܛ̈ܗܐ ܘܐܡܠܝܘܗܝ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ. ܝܕܥܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܐ ܠܝ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܟܘܠܬܐ. ܘܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܬܩܢܬ ܩܕܡܝ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܬܝܒܘܬܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܥܕ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܬܒܥ ܡܢ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܢܩܪ̈ܒܘܢ ܠܝ ܕܝܠܟ ܓܝܪ ܡܣܬܒܪ̈ܢ ܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ. ܘܕܝܠܗܿ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ. ܠܘܬܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܠܝ ܠܘ ܗܟܢܝܐ ܗܝ ܐܠܐ ܠܪܘܪܒܬܟ ܚܶܫܒ ܐܢܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܡܣܟܢܘܬܗ ܕܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܟ. ܘܠܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬܗܿ ܡܢܝܐ ܐܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܕܚܘܒܗܿ ܀
ran and approached me at your place. And, while she knew that she was going to be accused by you, she despised your accusations against her and she even braved the mockery of the spectators. She made little of the reproaches of the guests and despised those who at the doors were pushing her back. 42 In all this, she profited from the boldness that the love she possessed (gave her), and as soon as she had dared to enter near me, she brought me what I love and having set before me the table of her soul, not so that I might use it, but so that I might deliver her from (her) sins and fill her with forgiveness. For she knew that a table of food is not worthy of me; so she prepared before me a table of repentance, the one that I am accustomed to asking men to offer me. For what you possess, you, by men is esteemed greatness, and what she has, smallness; but, in my eyes, it is not so, for what I possess. Your greatness, I esteem as smallness, because of the poverty of your discernment; and her smallness, I count as greatness, because of the value of her love.
ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܒܡܫܚܐ ܚܳܐܪ ܐܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܒܨܒܝܢܗ̇ ܕܐܫܟܚܗ̇ ܠܗܕܐ. ܘܠܘ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܝܗ̇ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܒܩܐ ܐܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܒܢܝܫܐ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ ܐܫܕܐ ܠܗܝܢ. ܘܠܘ ܥܠ ܕܫܪܬ ܣܥܪܗ̇ ܘܟܦܪܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܡܫܒܚ ܐܢܐ ܠܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܡܿܩܠܣ ܐܢܐ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܬܗܿ ܕܠܘܬܝ ܕܡܢܐ ܐܫܟܚܬܿ. ܐܟܪ̈ܙܬ̇ ܓܝܪ ܒܥܒܿܕܐ ܢܣܝܢܐ ܕܚܘܒܗܿ. ܕܐܠܘ ܐܫܟܚܬܿ ܙܒܢܐ ܠܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܦܝ ܡܣܗܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܗܿܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܢ ܫܡܥܐ ܗܝܡܢܬܿ ܒܝ ܘܟܦܪܬ̇ ܒܢܝ̈ܚܝܗܿ. ܘܫܚܠܦܬܿ ܢܦܫܗ̇ ܡܢ ܡܕܡ ܠܡܕܡ ܡܢ ܛܒ̈ܐ ܕܥܠܝ ܫܡܥܬܿ ܕܡܬܡܠܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܐܝܟܢܝܐ ܠܐ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܡܠܦܢܘܬܝ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܬܐ ܆ ܢܦܫܗ̇ ܚܠܦܝ ܠܡܘܬܐ ܣܝܿܡܐ
43 For I do not look at (her) perfume, but at her good will that I find in her; I do not consider only her tears, but the intention for which she shed them; I do not praise her for having undone her hair and wiped my feet, but I congratulate (her) for her love for me, for what she knew how to find: for she proclaimed by an act the proof of her love, to the point that, if she found the occasion to testify for me, she would testify. It is indeed by hearsay that she believed in me and that she renounced her pleasures. She changed herself completely, following the fame of which she heard tell regarding me. How, not being at all within reach of my teaching, did she offer herself for me to death?
ܗܘܬ ܀ ܟܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܪ̈ܚܿܡܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܆ ܘܥܠ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܣܖܬ ܡܛܠ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬܝ. ܟܡܐ ܝܘܬܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܢ̈ܫܝܐ ܡܢ ܐܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܡܟܢܫܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܘܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܫܒܬ ܚܘܣܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܝ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܬܐܬܪ̈. ܟܡܐ ܢܝ̈ܚܐ ܘܦܘܪ̈ܦܥܐ ܦܓܖ̈ܢܐ ܡܕܒܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܆ ܘܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܣܠܝܬ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝ ܬܫܦܪ. ܟܡܐ ܡܬܥܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܥܠܡܐ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܘܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܟܦܖܪܬ ܡܛܘܠ ܕܒܝ ܬܘܕܐ. ܟܡܐ ܛܘ̈ܒܐ ܡܩܒܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܕܡܬܚܙܐ ܛܘܒܐ ܚܫܝܒ ܠܗܘܢ ܆ ܘܟܠܗܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܣܬܒܪ̈ܢ ܛܘܒܐ. ܕܘܝܐ ܐܢܝܢ
44 As many friends as she had had, she despised them all for love of me. As many human profits as she had gathered from her salary as a prostitute, she esteemed them all a loss to inherit only from me. As many bodily pleasures and enjoyments as she procured for herself, she rejected them all to please me. As much as the world had been attached to her with all its goods, she denied them all to confess me. As many advantages as she received from those for whom the visible world is esteemed a good, everything that is esteemed an advantage was judged by her miserable, to attain the gift of beatitude coming from me.
ܐܬܚܫܒ ܠܗܿ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܛܘܒܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܘܬܝ ܬܡܢܥ ܀ ܟܡܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܗܘܬ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ̇ ܒܢܝ̈ܚܝܗܿ. ܘܫܚܠܦܬܗ̇ ܠܚܫܐ ܘܠܟܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܪ̈ܓܫܬ ܒܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܐ ܕܡܟܪܙ ܝܘܠܦܢܝ. ܟܡܐ ܚܒܝܒ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܦܓܪܐ ܒܢܝ̈ܚܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܗܿ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܐܪܓܫܬ ܕܕܢܗܘܐ ܐܠܝܨ ܦܓܖܐ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܐ. ܛܝܒܬܗ ܕܢܣܝܒܪ ܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܐ ܕܚܠܦܝ.
45 How the joy of her soul abounded in her pleasures! But she transformed it into suffering and affliction, because she felt the trials that my teaching proclaimed. How dear her body was to her, more than her soul, in her pleasures! But, because she had the feeling that I was asking


[p. 512] {#po.41.p512}
ܟܡܐ ܥܣܝܩ ܠܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܫܘܚܠܦܐ ܕܥܝ̈ܕܐ ܥܬܝ̈ܩܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܝܬܪܬ ܒܚܘܒܐ. ܙܟܬ ܒܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܠܓܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܥܝܕܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ. ܕܟܡܐ ܡܝܬܪܬ ܘܙܕܝܩܬ ܡܢܗ̇ ܣܒܼܪܬ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܟ ܘܡܕܡ ܕܡܢܗܿ ܐܣܬܥܪ ܠܘܬܝ ܠܐ ܚܘܹܝܬ ܆ ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ. ܕܫܒܝܩܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܚܛ̈ܗܝܗ̇ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܀
that the body be mortified, she prepared it to endure trials for me. How difficult it is for men to change their old habits! But because she abounded in love, she triumphed with discernment over the tenacity of the habits (of a life) of laxity. As she has made progress and has become just, do you think as much of your soul? What is accomplished by her near me, she does not make a display of. It is
ܐܢܬ ܓܝܪ ܙܡܢܬܢܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܪ̈ܚܿܡܐ. ܘܣܦܩܬ ܠܟ ܗܕܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܘܠܐ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܪ̈ܓܠܐ ܝܗܼܒܬ. ܘܠܐ ܪܺܝܫܝ ܒܡܫܚܐ ܨܒ̇ܥܬ. ܘܠܐ ܫܠܡܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܝܗܒܬ ܠܝ ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܬ̇ ܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܪ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܓܠܝ ܨܒܥܬ̇ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܝܗܿ. ܘܣܥܖܪܗ̇ ܣܕܘܢܐ ܥܒܕܬ̇ ܠܗ̈ܝܢ. ܘܒܡܫܚܐ ܕܒܣܡܐ ܡܫܚܬ ܐܠܝ̈ܢ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܠܘ ܣܟܣ ܕܚܘܒܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܬܚܘܝܬܗ ܕܚܘܒܗܿ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ. ܕܫܒܝܩܝܢ ܠܗ ܚܛ̈ܗܝܗ̇ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܠܐ ܬܒܥܬܢܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܬܠ. ܘܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܠܦܢܐ ܫܐܠܬܢܝ. ܘܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܢܒܝܐ ܒܥܼܝܬ ܕܬܐܠܦ ܡܢܝ ܟܣ̈ܝܬܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܬܒܿܥܐ ܠܝ ܒܫܬܩܗܿ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܢܫ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܡܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܒܥܢܝ. ܘܡܟܪܙܐ ܠܝ ܒܫܬܩܗ̇ ܆ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܠ ܫܠܝܛ. ܡܕܡ ܕܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܝ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܒܥܐܘܢܝ ܆ ܗܕܐ ܙܢܝܬܐ ܒܥܝܐ ܠܝ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܙܕܝ̈ܩܘܗܝ ܕܥܡܐ ܠܐ ܫܐܠܘܢܝ ܆ ܗܕܐ ܚ̇ܛܝܬܐ ܫܐܠܐ ܠܝ ܀ ܟܡܐ ܬܕܡܪ̈ܬܐ ܥܒܿܕܬ ܩܕܡ ܒܢܰܝ ܥܡܟ ܘܩܕܡܝܟ. ܘܡܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܠܐ ܒܥܝܬܘܢܢܝ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܛܒܐ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܠܦܗܿ ܥܠܝ. ܘܡܢ ܡܠܬܐ ܩܒܠܬ̇ ܫܡܥܐ ܕܬܕܡܪ̈ܬܝ. ܗܐ ܬܒ̇ܥܐ ܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܫܘܒܩܢܐ ܕܚܛܗ̈ܝܗܿ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܕܫܒܝܩܝܢ ܠܗܿ ܚܛܗ̈ܝܗܿ
why I say to you: Her many sins are forgiven her. 46 For you, you invited me as one does to a friend, and, of that only you were content: water for my feet, you did not give me; perfume for my head, you did not honor me; the greeting of friendship, you did not give me. This one on the contrary approached me as for a Lord: my feet, she honored them with her tears; with her hair, she made a cloth for them and she anointed them with perfumed oil: her love was not limited to these actions, but they were the proof of her love. That is why I say to you: her many sins are forgiven her. 47 You, you did not ask me what I have to give, you did not question me, as one does with a Master; you did not pray to me, as one does with a prophet, to learn from me hidden things. But this (woman) by her silence asked me what no one yet among men had asked me, and by her silence she proclaimed me the all-powerful God. What until then my disciples had not asked me, this prostitute asks me. What the just of the people had not, from me, solicited, this sinner, from me, solicits. 48 How many miracles I have done before the sons of your people and before you, and you have asked me for nothing as (one asks) God! But this one regarding me learned only the good (pure and) simple: by a word she heard tell of my miracles; and behold she asks me, as God, for the forgiveness of her sins. That is why I say to you: her many sins are forgiven her.
ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܟܕ ܟܪܝܗܬ ܒܝܢܫ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܠܟ .ܘܟܕ ܡܡܚܝܐ ܢܦܫܟ ܒܫܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܘܬܐ ܘܫܘܒܗܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܫܘܡܬܐ ܡܣܼܒܪ ܐܢܬ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܨܐܘܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܗ̇ ܒܦܓܪܗ̇ ܗܝ. ܘܣܘܪ̈ܚܢܝܗܿ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܒܗܕܡ̈ܝܗܿ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ. ܚܛ̈ܝܬܗ ܙܥܘܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܚܫܝ̈ܒܢ ܠܗ̇ ܀ ܐܢܬ ܟܕ ܚܡܫܡܐܐ ܚܝܿܒ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܐܦܠܐ ܕܚܡܫܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܚܝܳܒܐ ܣܿܒܪ ܐܢܬ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܒܡܢܝܢܐ ܕܚܡܫܝܢ ܡܬܚܫܒܢ ܣܟ̈ܠܘܬܗ̇ ܆ ܡܛܘܠ ܕܠܘܬ ܦܓܪܐ ܐܢܝܢ. ܐܝܟ ܗܿܘ ܕܚܡܫܡܐܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܕܚܳܠܐ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܬܗܿ. ܡܪ̈ܓܫܢܘܬܐ ܓܝܖܿ ܕܚܛܝܬܗܿ ܐܣܓܝܬܗܿ ܠܘܬܗܿ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܙܓܫܢܘܬܟ ܕܝܠܟ ܕܒܣܦ̈ܘܬܟ ܥܒܕܬܟ ܕܬܤܒܪ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܟ ܙܕܝܩܐ ܀
49 You, while you are sick with grave passions, they do not appear so to you; while your soul is wounded by the wounds of pride and arrogance, you do not even imagine having a wound. But this (woman), while the defilement of her sin is (only) in her body and her defilements appear only in her members, her sins, (although) small, she esteems grave. 50 You, you owe five hundred (denarii) and you do not even believe yourself a debtor of fifty.
